F_{n}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\subset C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

4.4.
Duality between DG-algebras and DG-coalgebras

Denote by
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}
the category of DG-algebras over
k
(with
the usual DG-algebra homomorphisms).
Furthermore, let
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}
denote the category of augmented
DG-algebras (with DG-algebra homomorphisms preserving
the augmentations).

Similarly, denote by
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}
the category of DG-coalgebras
over
k
, and by
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{coaug}}
the category of coaugmented
DG-coalgebras.
Let
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}\subset k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{coaug}}
denote the full
subcategory whose objects are the conilpotent DG-coalgebras.

One easily observes that the cobar-construction
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

of any coaugmented DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is naturally an augmented
DG-algebra (with the direct summand projection
\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\longrightarrow(C^{+})^{\otimes 0}=k
providing the augmentation).
The bar-construction
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
of any augmented
DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is not only naturally coaugmented, but even
a conilpotent DG-coalgebra (essentially, because
the tensor coalgebra is conilpotent; see
Remark
3.2
).

So the bar-construction is a functor

\Br_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muk{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}},

while the cobar-construction is a functor

\Cb_{\gamma}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{coaug}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muk{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}.

Lemma 4.1
.

The restriction of the cobar-construction to conilpotent
DG-coalgebras,

\Cb_{\gamma}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muk{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}

is naturally a left adjoint functor to the bar-construction

\Br_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}
.

Proof.

Essentially, the assertion holds because
T(V)
is the free (graded)
algebra spanned by a (graded) vector space
V
, while
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(V)
is
the cofree conilpotent (graded) coalgebra cospanned by
V
.
A more detailed explanation, based on the notion of a twisting cochain,
will be offered in
Section
5.2
below.
∎

One would like to define equivalence relations (i. e., the classes of
morphisms to be inverted) in the categories of augmented DG-algebras
and conilpotent DG-coalgebras so that, after inverting these classes
of morphisms, the adjoint functors
\Br_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
\Cb_{\gamma}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

become equivalences of categories.
One important obstacle is that it is
not
good enough to
just invert the usual quasi-isomorphisms in both the categories

k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}
and
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}
, for the reason demonstrated
by Example
1.1
.

The problem is resolved by using the concept of a
filtered
quasi-isomorphism
introduced by Hinich in
[
30
, Section 4]
.
In the terminology of
[
30
]
, an
admissible filtration

on a coaugmented DG-coalgebra
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
is an exhaustive
comultiplicative increasing filtration by subcomplexes
F_{n}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

such that
F_{-1}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=0
and
F_{0}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\gamma(k)
.
This means that
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}F_{n}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and

\mu(F_{n}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset\sum_{p+q=n}^{p,q\geq 0}F_{p}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes F_{q}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

for every
n\geq 0
.

In particular, any coaugmented DG-coalgebra admitting an admissible
filtration is conilpotent.
Conversely, the canonical increasing filtration on any conilpotent
DG-coalgebra is admissible.

Let
f\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a morphism of conilpotent
DG-coalgebras.
The morphism
f
is said to be a
filtered quasi-isomorphism

if there exist admissible filtrations
F
on both the DG-coalgebras

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
such that
f(F_{n}C)\subset F_{n}D
and the induced map

F_{n}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}/F_{n-1}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow F_{n}D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}/F_{n-1}D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
k
-vector spaces
for every
n\geq 0
.

Notice that there is
no
reason to expect that the composition
of filtered quasi-isomorphisms should be a filtered quasi-isomorphism.
Nevertheless, leaving set-theoretical issues aside, to any class of
morphisms
\mathsf{S}
in a category
\mathsf{C}
one can assign the category

\mathsf{C}[\mathsf{S}^{-1}]
obtained by formally inverting all morphisms from
\mathsf{S}
;
in particular, one can formally invert all filtered quasi-isomorphisms
of DG-coalgebras.

By contrast, a morphism of DG-algebras
f\colon A\longrightarrow B
is said to be
a quasi-isomorphism if it is a quasi-isomorphism of the underlying
complexes of vector spaces.
Clearly, for any pair of composable morphisms of DG-algebras
f

and
g
, if two of the morphisms
f
,
g
, and
gf
are
quasi-isomorphisms, then so is the third one.

The following theorem goes back to
[
30
, Theorem 3.2]

and
[
47
, Théorème 1.3.1.2]
.
In the stated form, it can be found in
[
64
, Theorem 6.10(b)]
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
\mathsf{Quis}
be the class of all quasi-isomorphisms of augmented
DG-algebras and
\mathsf{FQuis}
be the class of all filtered
quasi-isomorphisms of conilpotent DG-coalgebras over
k
.
Then the adjoint functors
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}
and
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}

induce mutually inverse equivalences of categories

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}[\mathsf{Quis}^{-1}]\,\simeq\,k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}[\mathsf{FQuis}^{-1}]\,:\!\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}.

∎

Remarks 4.3
.

Clearly, any filtered quasi-isomorphism of DG-coalgebras is
a quasi-isomorphism of their underlying complexes of vector spaces
(i. e., a quasi-isomorphism in the usual sense of the word).
By the converse is
not
true.

The functor
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}

takes quasi-isomorphisms to filtered quasi-isomorphisms.
In particular, it follows that it takes quasi-isomorphisms to
quasi-isomorphisms.

The functor
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}\colon k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}

takes filtered quasi-isomorphisms to quasi-isomorphisms.
This important property of filtered quasi-isomorphisms of DG-coalgebras
is a part of Theorem
4.2
.

However, the functor
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}
does
not
take
quasi-isomorphisms to quasi-isomorphisms, generally speaking.
For example, let
f\colon(A,\alpha)\longrightarrow(B,\beta)
be a morphism of
augmented algebras (or augmented DG-algebras) as in
Example
1.1
, that is,
f
is

not
a quasi-isomorphism, but
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(f)
is.
Put
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
,
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\beta}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
,
and
g=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(f)
; so
g\colon(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)\longrightarrow(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\delta)
is
a quasi-isomorphism (but not a filtered quasi-isomorphism!) of
conilpotent DG-coalgebras.

It is a part of Theorem
4.2

that the adjunction morphism
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\eta}(\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\varepsilon}(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\longrightarrow E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a quasi-isomorphism of DG-algebras for any
augmented DG-algebra
(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\varepsilon)
.
So the adjunction morphisms
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\delta}(\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\beta}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

are quasi-isomorphisms.
It follows that
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(g)\colon\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\delta}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
not
a quasi-isomorphism
of DG-algebras.

4.5.
Quillen equivalence between DG-algebras and DG-coalgebras

The derived Koszul duality and comodule-contramodule correspondence
results can be usually expressed in the language of Quillen
equivalences between model categories
[
64
, Section 8.4]
.
In particular, the Koszul duality between augmented DG-algebras and
conilpotent DG-coalgebras stated in
Theorem
4.2
can be expressed
as a Quillen equivalence.
Let us briefly sketch the related assertions.

We suggest the book
[
33
]
as a standard reference source on
model categories and related concepts.
The category
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}
of DG-algebras over
k
has a standard
model category structure in which the quasi-isomorphisms are the weak
equivalences and the surjective DG-algebra maps (i. e.,
the DG-algebra maps that are surjective as maps of graded vector
spaces) are the fibrations
[
29
,
37
]
.
It is clear from the explicit descriptions of the classes of
cofibrations and trivial cofibrations in
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}
, which can be
found in the formulation of
[
64
, Theorem 9.1(a)]
, that
the model category
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}
is cofibrantly generated.
The similar assertions hold for the category of augmented DG-algebras

k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}

[
64
, Theorem 9.1(b)]
.

The category
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}
of conilpotent DG-coalgebras
over
k
also has a natural model
structure
[
30
, Theorem 3.1]
,
[
47
, Théorème 1.3.1.2(a)]
,

[
64
, Theorem 9.3(b)]
.
The weak equivalences can be described as the morphisms which get
inverted when one inverts the filtered quasi-isomorphisms.
The cofibrations are the injective DG-coalgebra maps (i. e.,
the DG-coalgebra maps that are injective as maps of graded vector
spaces).
Explicit descriptions of all the classes of morphisms involved
can be found in the formulation of
[
64
, Theorem 9.3(b)]
.

The model category
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}
is likewise cofibrantly
generated.
In fact, the injective morphisms of finite-dimensional DG-coalgebras
are the generating cofibrations (as one can see from the fact that
any DG-coalgebra is the directed union of its finite-dimensional
DG-subcoalgebras;
cf. Section
3.2
).
The injective filtered quasi-isomorphisms strictly compatible with
the filtrations, acting between finite-dimensional DG-coalgebras,
are the generating trivial cofibrations (this follows from
the description of the trivial cofibrations as the retracts of
the injective filtered quasi-isomorphisms strictly compatible with
the filtrations, as per
[
64
, Theorem 9.3(b)]
).

The pair of adjoint functors in
Lemma
4.1
and
Theorem
4.2
is a Quillen
equivalence between
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--alg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{aug}}
and
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--coalg}}}_{\mathsf{dg}}^{\mathsf{conilp}}

[
30
, Theorem 3.2]
,
[
47
, Théorème 1.3.1.2(b)]
,

[
64
, end of Section 9.3]
.

5.
Twisting Cochains

The concept of a
twisting cochain
explains the adjunction of
Lemma
4.1
and the constructions
of inverse equivalences in
Theorems
2.1
,

2.4
,

2.6
,
and
2.7
.
More generally, the notion of an
acyclic twisting cochain
,
defined in this section, allows to conveniently formulate
the Koszul duality theorems in wide contexts.

5.1.
The Hom DG-algebra and twisting cochains

Let
C
be a coassociative coalgebra and
A
be an associative
algebra over a field
k
.
Then the vector space
\Hom_{k}(C,A)
of all
k
-linear maps

C\longrightarrow A
acquires an associative
k
-algebra structure.
Given two linear maps
f
,
g\in\Hom_{k}(C,A)
, their product

fg\in\Hom_{k}(C,A)
is constructed as the composition

C\overset{\mu}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}C\otimes_{k}C\overset{f\otimes g}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}A\otimes_{k}A\overset{m}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}A,

where
\mu\colon C\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}C
is the comultiplication and

m\colon A\otimes_{k}A\longrightarrow A
is the multiplication map.
Given a counit
\epsilon\colon C\longrightarrow k
on
C
and a unit
e\colon k\longrightarrow A

in
A
, the unit element
1\in\Hom_{k}(C,A)
is constructed as
the composition
C\overset{\epsilon}{\longrightarrow}k\overset{e}{\longrightarrow}A
.

Given two graded vector spaces
U
and
V
over
k
, one denotes
by
\Hom_{k}(U,V)
the graded Hom space.
So
\Hom_{k}(U,V)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{k}^{i}(U,V)
, where

\Hom_{k}^{i}(U,V)
is the vector space of all homogeneous
k
-linear
maps
U\longrightarrow V
of degree
i
.
Then, for any graded coalgebra
C
and any graded algebra
A
,
the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(C,A)
is a graded algebra over
k
.

Given two complexes of vector spaces
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(U,V)
is endowed with a differential
in the usual way, producing the complex
\Hom_{k}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
For any DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and a DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
k
,
the complex
\Hom_{k}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a DG-algebra.

Let
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(E,d)
be a DG-algebra.
A homogeneous element
a\in E^{1}
of degree
1
in
E
is said to be
a
Maurer–Cartan element
(or a
Maurer–Cartan cochain
)
if it satisfies the
Maurer–Cartan equation

a^{2}+d(a)=0

in
E^{2}
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-coalgebra and
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-algebra over
k
.
By the definition, a
twisting cochain

\tau
for
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

and
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a Maurer–Cartan cochain in the DG-algebra

\Hom_{k}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
So
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a homogeneous
k
-linear map of
degree
1
(i. e.,
\tau_{i}\colon C^{i}\longrightarrow A^{i+1}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
)
satisfying the Maurer–Cartan equation.

Various conditions of compatibility with (co)augmentations are usually
imposed on twisting cochains.
In particular, let
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
be a coaugmented DG-coalgebra and

(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
be an augmented DG-algebra.
Twisting cochains
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
such that

\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
will be important for us
in this Section
5
.

5.2.
Bar-cobar adjunction and acyclic twisting cochains

We start with some examples before passing to the general case.

Examples 5.1
.

(1) Let
(A,\alpha)
be an augmented algebra over
k
,
and let
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
be its bar-construction,
as in Section
1.1
.
Let
\gamma\colon k\longrightarrow C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be the natural coaugmentation of

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
(as in
Section
4.4
).
Then the composition
\tau
of the direct summand projection

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)\longrightarrow A^{+}{}^{\otimes 1}=A^{+}
and the inclusion

A^{+}\longrightarrow A
is a twisting cochain for the DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

and the algebra
A
(viewed as a DG-algebra in the obvious way).
The equations of compatibility with the augmentation and
the coaugmentation
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
are satisfied
in this example.

(2) More generally, let
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
be an augmented DG-algebra,
and let
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
be its bar-construction,
as in Section
4.1
.
Denote by
\gamma\colon k\longrightarrow C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
the natural coaugmentation of

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
Then the composition
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
of the direct summand
projection
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow A^{{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},+}{}^{\otimes 1}=A^{{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},+}

and the inclusion
A^{{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},+}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a twisting cochain for
the DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The equations
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
are satisfied
for this twisting cochain.

Examples 5.2
.

(1) Let
(C,\gamma)
be a coaugmented coalgebra over
k
,
and let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
be its cobar-construction,
as in Section
2.6
.
Let
\alpha\colon A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow k
be the natural augmentation of

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
(as in
Section
4.4
).
Then the composition
\tau
of the natural surjection
C\longrightarrow C^{+}

and the direct summand inclusion
C^{+}\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)

is a twisting cochain for the coalgebra
C
(viewed as a DG-coalgebra
in the obvious way) and the DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The equations of compatibility with the augmentation and
the coaugmentation
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
are satisfied
in this example.

(2) More generally, let
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
be a coaugmented
DG-coalgebra, and let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
be
its cobar-construction, as in
Section
4.2
.
Denote by
\alpha\colon A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow k
the natural augmentation of

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
Then the composition
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
of the natural
surjection
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow C^{{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},+}
and the direct summand inclusion

C^{{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},+}\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a twisting cochain for
the DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The equations
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
are satisfied
for this twisting cochain.

Example 5.3
.

Let
(A,F,\alpha)
be an augmented nonhomogeneous quadratic algebra,
as defined in
Section
2.5
,
and let
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\subset\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
be the related (nonhomogeneous
quadratic dual) DG-subcoalgebra.
Then the composition
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)\longrightarrow A
of
the inclusion map
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
with
twisting cochain
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)\longrightarrow A
from
Example
5.1
(1) is
a twisting cochain
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A
.
Equivalently, the twisting cochain
\tau
can be constructed as
the composition of the direct summand projection
C\longrightarrow C^{1}=V=F_{1}A/F_{0}A
and the inclusion
F_{1}A/F_{0}A\simeq A^{+}\cap F_{1}A\longrightarrow A
.

The natural coaugmentation
k\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
of
the cobar DG-algebra
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
factorizes as

k\longrightarrow C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
, making
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

a coaugmented (in fact, conilpotent) DG-coalgebra with
the coaugmentation
\gamma\colon k\longrightarrow C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The twisting cochain
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A
satisfies
the equations
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
.

Now we can return to the proof of
Lemma
4.1
.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
:
further details.

Let
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
be an augmented DG-algebra and
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)

be a conilpotent DG-coalgebra.
Then the underlying graded coalgebra of the bar-construction

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
\Br_{\alpha}(A)=\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A^{+}[1])
and
the underlying graded algebra of the cobar-construction

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
\Cb_{\gamma}(C)=T(C^{+}[-1])
.

Since
T(C^{+}[-1])
is the free graded algebra spanned by
C^{+}[-1]
,
graded algebra homomorphisms
\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\longrightarrow A
correspond
bijectively to homogeneous
k
-linear maps
C^{+}[-1]\longrightarrow A
of
degree
0
.
Among these, the graded algebra homomorphisms compatible with
the augmentations on
\Cb_{\gamma}(C)
and
A
correspond precisely
to the linear maps
C^{+}[-1]\longrightarrow A^{+}
.
This means elements of the vector space
\Hom_{k}^{1}(C^{+},A^{+})
.

Since
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A^{+}[1])
is the cofree conilpotent graded coalgebra
cospanned by
A^{+}[1]
and
C
is a conilpotent graded coalgebra,
graded coalgebra homomorphisms
C\longrightarrow\Br_{\alpha}(A)
correspond
bijectively to homogeneous
k
-linear maps
C^{+}\longrightarrow A^{+}[1]

of degree
0
.
This means elements of the same vector space
\Hom_{k}^{1}(C^{+},A^{+})
.

Finally, one has to check that a graded algebra homomorphism

\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\longrightarrow A
commutes with the differentials on

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
and
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
if and only if the related
element of
\Hom_{k}^{1}(C^{+},A^{+})\subset\Hom_{k}^{1}(C,A)
satisfies
the Maurer–Cartan equation.
Similarly, a graded coalgebra homomorphism
C\longrightarrow\Br_{\alpha}(A)

commutes with the differentials on
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

if and only if the related element of
\Hom_{k}^{1}(C^{+},A^{+})\subset\Hom_{k}^{1}(C,A)
satisfies the same Maurer–Cartan equation.

To sum up, both the augmented DG-algebra homomorphisms

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the (conilpotent)
DG-coalgebra homomorphisms
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

correspond bijectively to twisting cochains
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

satisfying the equations of compatibility with the augmentation
and the coaugmentation
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
.
∎

Let
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
be an augmented DG-algebra,
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
be
a conilpotent DG-coalgebra, and
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be
a twisting cochain satisfying the equations
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
.
The twisting cochain
\tau
is said to be
acyclic
if one of
(or equivalently, both) the related DG-algebra homomorphism

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the DG-coalgebra
homomorphism
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
become isomorphisms
after the quasi-isomorphisms of DG-algebras, or respectively
the filtered quasi-isomorphisms of conilpotent DG-coalgebras, are
inverted, as in Theorem
4.2
.
Simply put,
\tau
is called acyclic if the related homomorphism
of DG-algebras
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
a quasi-isomorphism.

For example, the twisting cochain from
Examples
5.2

is acyclic, by the definition, whenever the coaugmented coalgebra

(C,\gamma)
or the coaugmented DG-coalgebra
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)

is conilpotent.
It is a part of Theorem
4.2

that the twisting cochain from
Examples
5.1

is acyclic for any augmented algebra
(A,\alpha)
or
an augmented DG-algebra
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
.

Concerning the twisting cochain
\tau
from
Example
5.3
,
it is acyclic for any (augmented) nonhomogeneous
Koszul

algebra
(A,F)
.
This claim is a corollary of the proof of Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt
theorem for nonhomogeneous quadratic/Koszul algebras
in
[
61
, Sections 3.2–3.3]

or
[
58
, Proposition 7.2(ii) in Chapter 5]
.

5.3.
Twisted differential on the tensor product

Let
(A,\alpha)
be an augmented algebra and
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of
left
A
-modules.
Let us make some basic observations about the bar-constructions

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
and
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A,M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
, as defined in
Sections
1.1

and
1.3
.

The underlying graded vector space
\Br_{\alpha}(A,M)
of the complex

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A,M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is isomorphic to the tensor product

\Br_{\alpha}(A)\otimes_{k}M
of the underlying graded vector spaces of
the complexes
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
and
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
But the differential on
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A,M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
not

the tensor product of the differentials on
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)

and
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Rather, in the formula for the differential of

\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A,M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
, there are the summands coming from
the differential of
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
, there is the summand coming
from the differential of
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, but there is also one additional
summand, defined in terms of the action of
A
in
M
.

Similarly, let
(C,\gamma)
be a coaugmented coalgebra and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be
a complex of left
C
-comodules.
Consider the cobar-constructions
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
and

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
, as defined in
Sections
2.6

and
2.7
.

The underlying graded vector space
\Cb_{\gamma}(C,N)
of the complex

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is isomorphic to the tensor product

\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\otimes_{k}N
of the underlying graded vector spaces of
the complexes
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
But the differential on
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
not

the tensor product of the differentials on
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)

and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Rather, in the formula for the differential of

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
, there are the summands coming from
the differential of
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
, there is the summand coming
from the differential on
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, but there is also one additional
summand, defined in terms of the coaction of
C
in
N
.

The construction of the twisted differential on the tensor product
of a DG-module and a DG-comodule (twisted by a twisting
cochain
\tau
) is abstracted from these observations.
Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-algebra and
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-coalgebra over
k
.
Let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a twisting cochain.

Let
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(M,d_{M})
be a right DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and let

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(N,d_{N})
be a left DG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Consider the tensor product
M\otimes_{k}N
of the underlying graded
vector spaces of
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and endow it with
the differential given by the formula

d(x\otimes y)=d_{M}(x)\otimes y+(-1)^{|x|}x\otimes d_{N}(y)\pm d^{\tau}(x\otimes y)

for all homogeneous elements
x\in M
and
y\in N
of
degrees
|x|
and
|y|
.
Here
d^{\tau}\colon M\otimes_{k}N\longrightarrow M\otimes_{k}N
is the composition

M\otimes_{k}N\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{k}C\otimes_{k}N\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{k}A\otimes_{k}N\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{k}N

of the map induced by the comultiplication map
N\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}N
,
the map induced by the twisting cochain
\tau\colon C\longrightarrow A
, and
the map induced by the multiplication map
M\otimes_{k}A\longrightarrow M
.
The reader can consult with
[
64
, Section 6.2]
for
the sign rule.

Then one can check that
d^{2}(x\otimes y)=0
.
So the tensor product of graded vector spaces
M\otimes_{k}N
endowed
with the differential
d
is a complex.
We denote this complex by
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Similarly, let
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(M,d_{M})
be a left DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
and let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(N,d_{N})
be a right DG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Consider the tensor product
N\otimes_{k}M
of the respective underlying
graded vector spaces, and endow it with the differential given by
the formula

d(y\otimes x)=d_{N}(y)\otimes x+(-1)^{|y|}y\otimes d_{M}(x)\pm d^{\tau}(y\otimes x),

where
d^{\tau}\colon N\otimes_{k}M\longrightarrow N\otimes_{k}M
is the composition

N\otimes_{k}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muN\otimes_{k}C\otimes_{k}M\overset{\tau}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}N\otimes_{k}A\otimes_{k}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muN\otimes_{k}M.

Once again, choosing the sign properly
(cf.
[
64
, Section 6.2]
), one can can check that

d^{2}(y\otimes x)=0
.
So the tensor product
N\otimes_{k}M
endowed with the differential
d

is a complex.
We denote this complex by
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

5.4.
Derived Koszul duality on the comodule side in
the augmented case

Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-algebra and
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-coalgebra
over
k
.
Suppose that we are given a twisting cochain
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Given a left DG-comodule
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, we consider
the complex of vector spaces
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
constructed
in Section
5.3
.
Then the structure of right DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
on
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has
been eaten up in the construction of the twisted differential on
the tensor product, but the left DG-module structure of
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is inherited by the twisted tensor product, making

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
a left DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Similarly, given a left DG-module
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, we consider
the complex of vector spaces
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
constructed
in Section
5.3
.
Then the right DG-comodule structure over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
on
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has
been consumed in the construction of the twisted differential on
the tensor product, but the left DG-comodule structure of
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is inherited by the twisted tensor product, making

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
a left DG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Denoting by
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
the DG-category of left DG-modules over

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and by
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
the DG-category of left DG-comodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, one observes that the DG-functor

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}\,\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muA^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

is left adjoint to the DG-functor

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}\,\colon A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}.

Similarly to
Section
2.7
,
we formulate two versions of the derived Koszul duality theorem,
a conilpotent and a nonconilpotent one.
Let
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
be an augmented DG-algebra and
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)

be a coaugmented DG-coalgebra.
Suppose that we are given a twisting cochain
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

satisfying the equations
\alpha\circ\tau=0=\tau\circ\gamma
.

Theorem 5.4
.

In the context of the previous paragraph, assume further that
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is a conilpotent DG-coalgebra (as defined in
Section
4.3
)
and
\tau
is an acyclic twisting cochain (as defined in
Section
5.2
).
Then the adjoint functors
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
induce a triangulated
equivalence between the conventional
derived
category of
left DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the
coderived
category of
left DG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}).

Proof.

This is a particular case of
[
64
, Theorem 6.5(a)]
;
see
[
47
, Théorème 2.2.2.2]
and
[
42
, Section 4]
for
an earlier approach.
The definition of the coderived category will be explained below
in Section
7.7
.
∎

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
be a coaugmented DG-coalgebra, and let

\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be the twisting
cochain constructed in
Example
5.2
(2).
Then the adjoint functors
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

and
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
induce a triangulated
equivalence between the
absolute derived
category of
left DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the
coderived
category of
left DG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}).

Proof.

This is a particular case of
[
64
, Theorem 6.7(a)]
.
For the definitions of the coderived and absolute derived categories,
see Sections
7.6
–
7.7
below.
The absolute derived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
coincides with
the coderived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

by
[
64
, Theorem 3.6(a)]
; see
Theorem
7.8
(a) below.
∎

In both Theorems
5.4

and
5.5
, the triangulated
equivalence takes the left DG-module
k
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
(with
the
A
-module structure on
k
defined in terms of
the augmentation
\alpha
) to the cofree left DG-comodule
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The same equivalences take the free left DG-module
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
to the left DG-comodule
k
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
(with
the
C
-comodule structure on
k
defined in terms of
the coaugmentation
\gamma
).

For generalizations of
Theorems
5.4

and
5.5
, see
Section
6.9
below.

6.
CDG-Rings and CDG-Coalgebras

Under Koszul duality, lack of chosen (co)augmentation on one side
corresponds to curvature on the other side.
For example, to a nonaugmented algebra a curved DG-coalgebra is
assigned.
Moreover, a change of (co)augmentation corresponds to a change of
flat connection, or in other words, to a Maurer–Cartan twist of
the DG-structure on the other side.
The aim of this section is to extend the Koszul duality theorems
of Sections
4.4

and
5.4
to
the nonaugmented context.

6.1.
Posing the problem of nonaugmented Koszul duality

Suppose that we are given an associative algebra
A
, and we would
like to compute the derived category of modules over it, in terms of
some kind of Koszul duality.
Following the approach of Sections
1

and
2.4
, we
have to choose an augmentation of
A
and produce the bar-construction

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A)
using an augmentation
\alpha\colon A\longrightarrow k
.

But an augmentation does not seem to be relevant to the problem of
describing
A
-modules or complexes of
A
-modules.
What if
A
does not admit an augmentation?
A
k
-algebra homomorphism
A\longrightarrow k
does not always exist.
Or if
A
has many possible augmentations, what is the point of
choosing one of them?

To be sure, the bar-complex
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}(A,M)
computes
the vector spaces
\Tor^{A}_{n}(k,M)
for a given
A
-module
M
,
as mentioned in Section
1.2
.
But one can have
\Tor^{A}_{n}(k,M)=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, while

M\neq 0
, as per Examples
1.2
.
This is the reason why the
coderived category
of DG-comodules,
rather than the conventional derived category, appears in
Theorem
2.1
.
What is the point of choosing an
A
-module structure on
k
and
considering the homological functor
\Tor^{A}_{*}(k,{-})
, only to
discover that this functor annihilates a big part of the desired
derived category
\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
?

The approach worked out in
[
64
]
and going back
to
[
61
]
can be briefly stated as follows.
Instead of looking for an augmentation
\alpha\colon A\longrightarrow k
, let us
choose an arbitrary
k
-linear map
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
for which
v(1)=1
.
Surely any nonzero
k
-algebra
A
admits plenty of such maps
v
.

Let us extend the bar-construction
\Br^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\alpha}
to the context
of arbitrary
k
-linear maps
v
as above.
Then the resulting object
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)
is not a DG-algebra, and

not even a complex
at all.
But it is a graded coalgebra
C
endowed with an odd coderivation

\partial\colon C^{n}\longrightarrow C^{n+1}
with a
nonzero
square.
The square of the differential
\partial
is described as the commutator
with the
curvature linear function

h\colon C^{-2}\longrightarrow k
.

The resulting algebraic object
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,\partial,h)
is called
a
curved DG-coalgebra
, or a
CDG-coalgebra
for brevity.
To any
A
-module
M
, we assign a
CDG-comodule

\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
; and similarly to any complex of

A
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The differential on
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
does
not
square to zero;
so the homology spaces of
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
(as well as of

\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)
) are
undefined
.
But we have already decided that we are not too much interested in
the Tor spaces
\Tor^{A}_{*}(k,M)
anyway.

Replacing the
k
-linear map
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
by another such map

v^{\prime}\colon A\longrightarrow k
, satisfying the same condition
v^{\prime}(1)=1
, leads
to a CDG-coalgebra
(C,d^{\prime},h^{\prime})
naturally isomorphic to
(C,d,h)
.
The DG-categories of CDG-comodules over
(C,d^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and

(C,d,h)
are isomorphic.

Finally, though one cannot speak of quasi-isomorphisms of
CDG-comodules in the conventional sense of the word, and therefore
the conventional derived category of CDG-comodules over
(C,d,h)

is
undefined
, the definition of the
coderived
category
makes perfect sense for CDG-comodules.
The problem of computing the derived category of
A
-modules is
solved by constructing a triangulated equivalence between the derived
category
\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
and the coderived category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
of CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
.

Unrelated to the Koszul duality theory, curved DG-modules also
appear in the literature in connection with the popular topic of

matrix factorizations
(which are rather special particular
cases of CDG-modules)
[
24
,
14
,
40
,
56
]
.
The coderived and absolute derived categories are an important
technical tool in the matrix factorization
theory
[
57
,
59
,
19
,
2
,
77
]
.
(Weakly) curved
\mathrm{A}_{\infty}
-algebras play a fundamental
role in the Fukaya theory
[
26
,
27
,
18
,
65
]
.

6.2.
CDG-rings and CDG-modules

The following definitions go back to the paper
[
61
]
.
The terminology “curvature” and “connection” comes from
an analogy with the respective concepts from differential geometry,
based on examples from differential geometry
[
61
, Section 4]
,

[
75
, Sections 10.2–10.8]
.
The latter class of examples, namely, the duality between the rings of
differential operators and (curved) DG-rings of differential forms,
is an instance of a more complicated
relative
version of
nonhomogeneous Koszul duality
[
39
]
,
[
6
, Section 7.2]
,

[
63
, Section 0.4 and Chapter 11]
,
[
64
, Appendix B]
,

[
75
]
, which falls outside of the scope of this survey.
We refer to the book
[
75
]
for a definitive treatment.

A
curved DG-ring
(
CDG-ring
)
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
is a graded
ring
B=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}B^{i}
endowed with the following data:

•

d\colon B\longrightarrow B
is an odd derivation of degree
1
, that is,
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
an additive map
d_{i}\colon B^{i}\longrightarrow B^{i+1}
is
given such that the Leibniz rule with signs

d(bc)=d(b)c+(-1)^{|b|}bd(c)

is satisfied for all homogeneous elements
b
and
c\in B
of
degrees
|b|
and
|c|
;

•

h\in B^{2}
is an element.

The following axioms relating
d
and
h
must be satisfied:

(i)

the square of
the differential
d
on
B
is described by the formula
d^{2}(b)=hb-bh

for all
b\in B
;

(ii)

d(h)=0
.

The element
h\in B^{2}
is called the
curvature element
.

A DG-ring is the same thing as a CDG-ring with
h=0
.
The category of DG-rings is a subcategory, but
not
a full
subcategory of the category of CDG-rings; the passage from
the uncurved to the curved DG-rings involves not only adding new
objects to the category, but also adding new morphisms between
previously existing objects.
Let us define the morphisms of CDG-rings now.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d_{B},h_{B})
and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A,d_{A},h_{A})
be two CDG-rings.
A
morphism of CDG-rings

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a pair
(f,a)
,
where

•

f\colon B\longrightarrow A
is a homomorphism of graded rings;

•

a\in A^{1}
is an element

such that

(iii)

f(d_{B}(z))=d_{A}(f(z))+[a,f(z)]
for all
z\in B
(where the
graded commutator
[{-},{-}]
is defined by the usual rule

[x,y]=xy-(-1)^{|x||y|}yx
for all homogeneous elements
x
and
y

of degrees
|x|
and
|y|
);

(iv)

f(h_{B})=h_{A}+d_{A}(a)+a^{2}
.

The element
a\in A^{1}
is called the
change-of-connection

element.

The composition of two morphisms of CDG-rings
(C,d_{C},h_{C})\longrightarrow(B,d_{B},h_{B})\longrightarrow(A,d_{A},h_{A})
is given by the rule
(f,a)\circ(g,b)=(f\circ g,\>a+f(b))
.
The identity morphism
(B,d_{B},h_{B})\longrightarrow(B,d_{B},h_{B})
is
the morphism
(\mathrm{id}_{B},0)
.

Morphisms of CDG-rings
(\mathrm{id}_{B},a)\colon(B,d^{\prime},h^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B,d,h)
are
called the
change-of-connection
morphisms.
All such morphisms of CDG-rings are isomorphisms.
Moreover, for any CDG-ring
(B,d,h)
and an element
a\in B^{1}

there exists a unique CDG-ring structure
(B,d^{\prime},h^{\prime})
on the graded
ring
B
such that
(\mathrm{id}_{B},a)\colon(B,d^{\prime},h^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B,d,h)
is
a (change-of-connection iso)morphism of CDG-rings.
The twisted differential and curvature element
d^{\prime}\colon B\longrightarrow B

and
h^{\prime}\in B^{2}
are given by the formulas
d^{\prime}(z)=d(z)+[a,z]
and

h^{\prime}=h+d(a)+a^{2}
.

In particular, one can assume that
h=0
; so
(B,d)
is a DG-ring.
Then one has
h^{\prime}=0
(i. e., the pair
(B,d^{\prime})
is a DG-ring again)
if and only if the equation
d(a)+a^{2}=0
is satisfied, i. e.,

a\in B^{1}
is a Maurer–Cartan element (in the sense of
Section
5.1
).
So any DG-ring structure can be twisted by any Maurer–Cartain
cochain, producing a new DG-ring structure on the same graded ring.
The resulting DG-ring
(B,d^{\prime})
is naturally isomorphic to
the original DG-ring
(B,d)

as a CDG-ring
, but not
as a DG-ring.

The following definition of a
CDG-module
can be found
in
[
58
, Section 4 of Chapter 5]
or
[
64
, Section 3.1]
.
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring.
A
left CDG-module

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M,d_{M})
over
(B,d,h)
is a graded
left
B
-module
M=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{i}
endowed with
the following datum:

•

d_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M
is an odd derivation of degree
1
compatible
with the odd derivation
d
on
B
, that is, for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}

an additive map
d_{M,i}\colon M^{i}\longrightarrow M^{i+1}
is given such that
the Leibniz rule with signs

d_{M}(bx)=d(b)x+(-1)^{|b|}bd_{M}(x)

is satisfied for all homogeneous elements
b\in B
and
x\in M
of
degrees
|b|
and
|x|
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(v)

the square of
the differential
d_{M}
on
M
is described by the formula
d_{M}^{2}(x)=hx

for all
x\in M
.

Similarly, a
right CDG-module

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N,d_{N})
over
(B,d,h)

is a graded right
B
-module
N=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}N^{i}
endowed with
the following datum:

•

d_{N}\colon N\longrightarrow N
is an odd derivation of degree
1
compatible
with the odd derivation
d
on
B
, i. e.,
d_{N}
acts on
the grading components of
N
as
d_{N,i}\colon N^{i}\longrightarrow N^{i+1}
and
the Leibniz rule with signs

d_{N}(yb)=d_{N}(y)b+(-1)^{|y|}yd(b)

is satisfied for all homogeneous elements
b\in B^{|b|}
and

y\in N^{|y|}
.

The following axiom is imposed:

(vi)

the square of
the differential
d_{N}
on
N
is given by the formula
d_{N}^{2}(y)=-yh

for all
y\in N
.

Comparing the equation for the square of the differential
in (
i
)
with (
v
)
and (
vi
), one observes that
they do not agree.
So
there is no natural left
(or
right
)
CDG-module
structure on the underlying graded ring
B
of a CDG-ring
(B,d,h)
.
However, any CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is naturally a
CDG-bimodule

over itself, in the sense of the definition
in
[
64
, Section 3.10]
or
[
75
, Section 6.1]
.

To any two (say, left) CDG-modules
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(L,d_{L})
and
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M,d_{M})

over a CDG-ring
(B,d,h)
, one can assign the
complex of
morphisms

\Hom_{B}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
from
L
to
M
.
The degree
n
component
\Hom_{B}^{n}(L,M)
of the complex

\Hom_{B}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is the group of all homogeneous
B
-linear
maps
L\longrightarrow M
of degree
n
(see
[
64
, Sections 1.1
and 3.1]
for the sign rule involved).
The differential
d
on the complex
\Hom_{B}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is
defined by the usual rule
d(f)(l)=d_{M}(f(l))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{L}(l))

for all
l\in L
.

The rule above is well-known to define a differential with zero square
on the graded abelian group of homogeneous morphisms between two

DG-modules
.
It turns out that for two CDG-modules over a CDG-ring, the same
formula still defines a differential with zero square, as two
curvature-related terms in the computation of
d^{2}(f)
cancel
each other.
Consequently, there is the
DG-category of left CDG-modules

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
, which we denote simply by
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Given a morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon(B,d_{B},h_{B})\longrightarrow(A,d_{A},h_{A})

and a left CDG-module
(M,d_{M})
over
(A,d_{A},h_{A})
, one can endow
the graded left
A
-module
M
with the induced structure of graded
left
B
-module and a twisted differential
d^{\prime}_{M}
, making
(M,d^{\prime}_{M})

a left CDG-module over
(B,d_{B},h_{B})
.
The twisted differential
d^{\prime}_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M
is defined by the formula

d^{\prime}_{M}(x)=d_{M}(x)+ax
for all
x\in M
.
Similarly, for a right CDG-module
(N,d_{N})
over
(A,d_{A},h_{A})
,
the twisted differential
d^{\prime}_{N}
defined by the rule

d^{\prime}_{N}(y)=d_{N}(y)-(-1)^{|y|}ya
for all
y\in N^{|y|}
makes
(N,d^{\prime}_{N})

a right CDG-module over
(B,d_{B},h_{B})
.

In particular, the DG-categories of CDG-modules over two isomorphic
CDG-rings are naturally isomorphic.
Specializing to CDG-isomorphisms (or in other words,
change-of-connection isomorphisms) of DG-rings, we come to
the (perhaps not too widely known) observation that

a Maurer–Cartan twist of the differential on a DG-ring
does not change the DG-category of DG-modules
.
The reason is that the differentials on DG-modules
(M,d_{M})
over
a DG-ring
(B,d)
can be twisted (by a Maurer–Cartan cochain

a\in B^{1}
) alongside with a twist
d\leadsto d^{\prime}=d+[a,{-}]
of
the differential on
B
(according to the rule above,

d^{\prime}_{M}(x)=d_{M}(x)+ax
).

6.3.
CDG-coalgebras and CDG-comodules

A
CDG-algebra

(B,d,h)
over a field
k
is a graded
(associative, unital)
k
-algebra endowed with a CDG-ring structure
with a
k
-linear differential
d
.
The definitions of a
CDG-coalgebra

(C,d,h)
and
a
CDG-comodule

(M,d_{M})
over it are obtained from those of
a CDG-algebra and a CDG-module by inverting the arrows.
Let us spell out some details.

Partly following the terminology of
[
44
]
, let us utilize the term

precomplex
for a graded vector space
K
endowed with
a
k
-linear differential
d\colon K\longrightarrow K
of degree
1

with
not necessarily zero square
.
A CDG-coalgebra
(C,d,h)
and a CDG-comodule
(M,d_{M})
are, first
of all, precomplexes: both
d\colon C\longrightarrow C
and
d_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M
are
homogeneous
k
-linear maps of degree
1
with, generally speaking,
nonzero squares.

Given two precomplexes
V^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(V,d_{V})
and
W^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(W,d_{W})
, one defines
their
tensor product

V^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes_{k}W^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(V\otimes_{k}W,\>d)
as follows.
The graded vector space
V\otimes_{k}W
is simply the tensor product of
the graded vector spaces
V
and
W
.
The differential
d
on
V\otimes_{k}W
is given by the usual rule

d(v\otimes w)=d_{V}(v)\otimes w+(-1)^{|v|}v\otimes d_{W}(w)
for
v\in V^{|v|}

and
w\in W^{|w|}
.

Now we can define the notions of
coderivations
on coalgebras
and comodules.
Let
C
be a graded coalgebra over
k
.
An
odd coderivation
(of degree
1
) on
C
is a homogeneous

k
-linear map
d\colon C\longrightarrow C
of degree
1
such that
the comultiplication map
\mu\colon C\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}C
is a morphism of
precomplexes (i. e.,
\mu
commutes with the differentials).
Here the differential
d
on
C\otimes_{k}C
is defined by the rule above.
One can check that any odd coderivation
d
on
C
is compatible with
the counit, in the sense that the counit
\epsilon\colon C\longrightarrow k
is
also a morphism of precomplexes (where the differential on
k
is zero).

Let
(C,d)
be a graded coalgebra endowed with an odd coderivation
of degree
1
, and let
M
be a graded left
C
-comodule.
Then an
odd coderivation
on
M

compatible with

the coderivation
d
on
C
is a
k
-linear map
d_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M

of degree
1
such that the left coaction map
\nu\colon M\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}M

is a morphism of precomplexes.
Here, once again, the differential
d
on
C\otimes_{k}M
is defined
by the rule above in terms of the differentials
d
on
C
and
d_{M}

on
M
.

In order to spell out the rules for the squares of the differentials

d
and
d_{M}
on a CDG-coalgebra
C
and a CDG-comodule
M
, we need
to have a brief preliminary discussion of algebras dual to coalgebras
and their actions in comodules.
It was already mentioned in
Section
3.2
that the dual vector
space to any coalgebra is an algebra.
Similarly, the graded dual vector space
C^{*}
to a graded coalgebra
C

is a graded algebra.
Furthermore, any graded left
C
-comodule
M
can be endowed with
a graded
C^{*}
-module structure with the action map defined as
the composition

C^{*}\otimes_{k}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC^{*}\otimes_{k}C\otimes_{k}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM

of the map induced by the left coaction map
\nu\colon M\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}M

and the map induced by the pairing map
C^{*}\otimes_{k}C\longrightarrow k
.

Let us pause at this point, however, and observe that for any
algebra
A
there is the opposite algebra
A^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Which one of the two opposite multiplications on
C^{*}
should one
choose and use as the canonical choice of the multiplication on
the dual vector space to a coalgebra?
The traditional way of making this choice
[
88
, Section 2.1]

results in
left

C
-comodules becoming
right

C^{*}
-modules and vice versa.
Our usual preference is to choose the sides so that left
C
-comodules
become left
C^{*}
-modules and right
C
-comodules become right

C^{*}
-modules (cf.
[
71
, beginning of Section 1.4]
).

With these observations in mind, let us introduce some notation.
Given a (graded) coalgebra
C
and two (homogeneous)
k
-linear
functions
a
,
b\colon C\longrightarrow k
(of some degrees
|a|
,
|b|\in\mathbb{Z}
),
we denote by
a*b\colon C\longrightarrow k
the product of
a
and
b
in
the graded algebra
C^{*}
.
Given a graded left comodule
M
over
C
, an element
x\in M
,
and a linear function
b\colon C\longrightarrow k
, we denote by
b*x\in M

the result of the left action of the element
b\in C^{*}
on
the element
x
in the left
C^{*}
-module
M
.
Similarly, for a graded right comodule
N
over
C
, a homogeneous
element
y\in N
, and a homogeneous linear function
b\colon C\longrightarrow k
,
we let
y*b\in N
denote the result of the right action of
b
on
y
.
We refer to
[
64
, Section 4.1]
for the sign rules.

Now we can dualize the definitions from
Section
6.2
.
A
curved DG-coalgebra
(
CDG-coalgebra
)
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)

over a field
k
is a graded coalgebra
C=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{i}

endowed with the following data:

•

d\colon C\longrightarrow C
is an odd coderivation of degree
1
(so
the grading components of
d
are
d_{i}\colon C^{i}\longrightarrow C^{i+1}
);

•

h\colon C\longrightarrow k
is a homogeneous linear function of degree
2

(so the only grading component of
h
is
h\colon C^{-2}\longrightarrow k
).

The following axioms relating
d
and
h
must be satisfied:

(i)

the square of
the differential
d
on
C
is described by the formula
d^{2}(c)=h*c-c*h

for all
c\in C
;

(ii)

h(d(c))=0
for all
c\in C^{-3}
.

The linear function
h\colon C^{-2}\longrightarrow k
is called
the
curvature linear function
.

A DG-coalgebra is the same thing as a CDG-coalgebra with
h=0
.
The category of DG-coalgebras is a subcategory, but
not

a full subcategory in the category of CDG-coalgebras, as one can
see from the following definition of a morphism of CDG-coalgebras.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d_{C},h_{C})
and
D^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(D,d_{D},h_{D})
be two CDG-coalgebras.
A
morphism of CDG-coalgebras

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow D^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
a pair
(f,a)
, where

•

f\colon C\longrightarrow D
is a homomorphism of graded coalgebras;

•

a\colon C\longrightarrow k
is a homogeneous linear function of degree
1

such that

(iii)

d_{D}(f(c))=f(d_{C}(c))+f(a*c)-(-1)^{|c|}f(c*a)
for all
homogeneous elements
c\in C^{|c|}
;

(iv)

h_{D}(f(c))=h_{C}(c)+a(d_{C}(c))+a^{*2}(c)
for all
c\in C
.

The linear function
a\colon C^{-1}\longrightarrow k
is called
the
change-of-connection linear function
.

The composition of morphisms and the identity morphisms in
the category of CDG-coalgebras are defined in the way similar/dual
to the one for CDG-rings (see
[
64
, Section 4.1]
for
the details).

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra.
A
left CDG-comodule

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M,d_{M})
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a graded
left
C
-comodule endowed with

•

an odd coderivation
d_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M
of degree
1
compatible
with the coderivation
d
on
C

such that

(v)

the square of
the differential
d_{M}
on
M
is described by the formula
d_{M}^{2}(x)=h*x

for all
x\in M
.

Similarly, a
right CDG-comodule

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N,d_{N})
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is a graded right
C
-comodule endowed with

•

an odd coderivation
d_{N}\colon N\longrightarrow N
of degree
1
compatible
with the coderivation
d
on
C

such that

(vi)

the square of
the differential
d_{N}
on
N
is given by the formula
d_{N}^{2}(y)=-y*h

for all
y\in N
.

Similarly to the theory of CDG-modules over CDG-rings discussed
in Section
6.2
, left CDG-comodules over
a CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
form a DG-category
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
.
Any morphism of CDG-coalgebras
(f,a)\colon(C,d_{C},h_{C})\longrightarrow(D,d_{D},h_{D})
induces a DG-functor
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow D^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}

assigning to a CDG-comodule
(M,d_{M})
the CDG-comodule
(M,d^{\prime}_{M})
,
with the graded
D
-comodule structure on
M
obtained from
the graded
C
-comodule structure on
M
by the corestriction
of scalars (as mentioned in
Section
3.1
) and
the twisted differential
d^{\prime}_{M}
given by the rule

d^{\prime}_{M}(x)=d_{M}(x)+a*x
.
So an isomorphism of CDG-coalgebras induces an isomorphism of
the DG-categories of CDG-comodules over them.

Similarly to Section
6.2
, the equations for
the square of the differential
in (
i
),
(
v
)
and (
vi
) are all different.
So a CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is naturally
neither
a left,

nor
a right CDG-comodule over itself; but it has a natural
structure of a
CDG-bicomodule

over itself
[
64
, Section 4.8]
.

6.4.
Nonaugmented bar-construction for algebras and modules

Let
A
be a nonzero associative
k
-algebra; so
1\in A
is
a nonzero element.
Consider the
k
-vector space
A_{+}=A/(k\cdot 1)

(cf. Section
1.1
).

Choose a
k
-linear map
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
such that
v(1)=1
.
So
v
is a
k
-linear retraction of the
k
-algebra
A
(viewed
as a
k
-vector space) onto its unit line
k=k\cdot 1\subset A
.
Put
V=\ker(v)\subset A
.
Then the composition of linear maps
V\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow A_{+}
is
an isomorphism, so the vector space
A_{+}
can be identified with
the subspace
V\subset A
, and
A=k\oplus V
as a
k
-vector space.

The multiplication map
m\colon A\otimes_{k}A\longrightarrow A
decomposes into
components according to the direct sum decomposition
A=k\oplus V
.
Notice that the restrictions of
m
to
k\cdot 1\otimes_{k}A
and

A\otimes_{k}k\cdot 1
are prescribed by the condition that
1\in A
is
a unit, so we do not need to keep track of these.
The restriction of the map
m
to
V\otimes_{k}V\subset A\otimes_{k}A

provides a linear map
V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow A=k\oplus V
.
Denote its components by
m_{V}\colon V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow V
and

m_{k}\colon V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow k
.

Consider the tensor coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{+}^{\otimes n}
, and endow it with the differential
\partial
,

k\overset{0}{\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu}A_{+}\overset{\partial}{\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu}A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\overset{\partial}{\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu}A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb,

given by the formulas
\partial(a\otimes b)=m_{V}(a\otimes b)
,
\partial(a\otimes b\otimes c)=m_{V}(a\otimes b)\otimes c-a\otimes m_{V}(b\otimes c)
, …,

\partial(a_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n})=m_{V}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})\otimes a_{3}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n}-\dotsb\\
+(-1)^{i+1}a_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{i-1}\otimes m_{V}(a_{i}\otimes a_{i+1})\otimes a_{i+2}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n}+\dotsb\\
+(-1)^{n}a_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n-2}\otimes m_{V}(a_{n-1}\otimes a_{n}),

etc., for all
a
,
b
,
c
,
a_{i}\in A_{+}
,
n\geq 1
, and

1\leq i\leq n-1
.
The identification
A_{+}\simeq V
is presumed here.
The leftmost differential
\partial\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow k
is the zero map.

Notice that the map
m_{V}\colon A_{+}\otimes A_{+}\longrightarrow A_{+}
is
not

an associative multiplication.
Accordingly, the tensor coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
with
the differential
\partial
is
not
a complex: one has
\partial^{2}\neq 0

(generally speaking).
Still, essentially by construction,
\partial
is an odd coderivation
of the graded coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
, in the sense of
Section
6.3
(to make it an odd coderivation
of degree
1
, one has to change the sign of the grading on
the tensor coalgebra).

Denote by
\Br(A)
the tensor coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
with the sign
of the grading changed, so
\Br^{-n}(A)=A_{+}^{\otimes n}
.
Furthermore, denote by
h\colon\Br^{-2}(A)\longrightarrow k
the linear map

-m_{k}\colon A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\longrightarrow k
.
Then
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)=(\Br(A),\partial,h)
is a CDG-coalgebra over
k
;
the linear function
h
is its curvature linear function.
This is the nonaugmented version of the bar-construction for
an associative algebra.

Now let
M
be a left
A
-module.
Consider the cofree left comodule
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})\otimes_{k}M
over
the tensor coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
, and endow it with
the differential
\partial
,

M\overset{\partial}{\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu}A_{+}\otimes_{k}M\overset{\partial}{\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu}A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\otimes_{k}M\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb,

given by the formulas
\partial(a\otimes x)=ax
,
\partial(a\otimes b\otimes x)=m_{V}(a\otimes b)\otimes\penalty x-a\otimes bx
, …,

\partial(a_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n}\otimes x)=m_{V}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})\otimes a_{3}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n}\otimes x-\dotsb\\
+(-1)^{n}a_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n-2}\otimes m_{V}(a_{n-1}\otimes a_{n})\otimes x\\
+(-1)^{n+1}a_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes a_{n-1}\otimes a_{n}x,

etc., for all
a
,
b
,
a_{i}\in A_{+}
,
x\in M
, and
n\geq 1
.
As above, the identification
A_{+}\simeq V
is presumed here
(explaining, in particular, the notation
ax
for the action of
an element
a\in A_{+}\simeq V
on an element
x\in M
).

Once again, the graded vector space
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})\otimes_{k}M
with
the differential
\partial
is
not
a complex.
Instead, the cofree graded comodule
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})\otimes_{k}M
over the graded
coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
, with the differential
\partial
on the cofree graded
comodule, is a left CDG-comodule over the CDG-coalgebra

\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)=(\Br(A),\partial,h)
.
We denote this CDG-comodule by
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
.
This is the nonaugmented version of the bar-construction for
a module over an associative algebra.

Now let
v^{\prime}\colon A\longrightarrow k
be another
k
-linear map such that

v^{\prime}(1)=1
.
Put
V^{\prime}=\ker(v^{\prime})\subset A
, and notice natural isomorphisms

V^{\prime}\simeq A_{+}\simeq V
.
The difference
v-\penalty v^{\prime}\colon A\longrightarrow k
is a
k
-linear map
taking
1
to
0
; so it factorizes through the natural surjection

A\longrightarrow A_{+}
, defining a
k
-linear map
l\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow k
.
The vector subspace
V^{\prime}\subset A
can be then described as

V^{\prime}=\{a^{\prime}=a+l(a)\mid a\in V\}\,\subset\,A.

Following the construction above, there are the linear maps

m_{V^{\prime}}\colon V^{\prime}\otimes_{k}V^{\prime}\longrightarrow V^{\prime}
and
m_{k}^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\otimes_{k}V^{\prime}\longrightarrow k

corresponding to the choice of the retraction
v^{\prime}\colon A\longrightarrow k

of the
k
-algebra
A
onto its unit line
k=k\cdot 1
.
Furthermore, there is the related differential
\partial^{\prime}
on the tensor
coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+})
and the related curvature linear function
h^{\prime}
,
forming together a CDG-coalgebra
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A)=(\Br(A),\partial^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

The linear function
l\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow k
can be viewed as
a change-of-connection linear function
l\colon\Br^{-1}(A)\longrightarrow k
.
Then one observes that the pair
(\mathrm{id},l)
is a change-of-connection
isomorphism of CDG-coalgebras
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A)
.

For any left
A
-module
M
, the differential
\partial^{\prime}
on
the bar-construction
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A,M)
is obtained from
the differential
\partial
on the bar-construction
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
by
twisting with the change-of-connection linear function
l
.
So the equivalence of DG-categories
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A){\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\simeq\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A){\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
induced by the isomorphism of CDG-coalgebras

(\mathrm{id},l)\colon\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A)
takes the CDG-comodule

\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A,M)
over
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)
to the CDG-comodule

\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A,M)
over
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(A)
.

Furthermore, given a homomorphism of associative algebras

f\colon A\longrightarrow B
, each of them endowed with a
k
-linear retraction
onto its unit
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
and
v^{\prime}\colon B\longrightarrow k
, the difference

v-v^{\prime}\circ f\colon A\longrightarrow k
defines a linear function
l\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow k
.
At the same time, the map
f
induces a
k
-linear map

f_{+}\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow B_{+}
, which in turn induces a homomorphism of
the tensor coalgebras
g\colon\Br(A_{+})\longrightarrow\Br(B_{+})
.
Then the pair
(g,l)
defines a morphism of CDG-coalgebras

(g,l)\colon\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A)\longrightarrow\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v^{\prime}}(B)
assigned to a morphism
of associative algebras
A\longrightarrow B
.
This makes the nonaugmented bar-construction functorial.

6.5.
Nonaugmented bar-construction for DG-algebras

Having spelled out the details of the nonaugmented bar-construction
in the simplest case of a
k
-algebra
A
, we will now briefly sketch
the generalization to DG-algebras
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The following construction is a nonaugmented version of
Section
4.1
.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(A,d)
be a DG-algebra over a field
k
.
Assume that
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\neq 0
, or equivalently,
1\in A^{0}
is a nonzero
element.
Notice that
k\cdot 1\subset A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a subcomplex, since
d(1)=0
.
Put
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{+}=A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}/(k\cdot 1)
; so
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{+}
is a complex of

k
-vector spaces, whose underlying graded vector space we denote
by
A_{+}
.

Choose a homogeneous
k
-linear map
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
of degree
0

such that
v(1)=1
.
So
v\colon A\longrightarrow k
is a homogeneous
k
-linear retraction of
the graded
k
-vector space
A
onto the unit line
k=k\cdot 1\subset A
.
Put
V=\ker(v)
; so
V
is a homogeneous
k
-vector subspace in
A

such that
A=k\oplus V
.
The composition
V\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow A_{+}
is an isomorphism of
graded
k
-vector spaces.

Both the multiplication map
m\colon A\otimes_{k}A\longrightarrow A
and the differential

d\colon A\longrightarrow A
decompose into components according to the direct
sum decomposition
A=k\oplus V
.
The restrictions of
m
to
k\cdot 1\otimes_{k}A
and
A\otimes_{k}k\cdot 1
are
prescribed by the condition that
1\in A
is a unit, while
the restriction of
d
to
k\cdot 1
vanishes; so we do not need to keep
track of these.
The restriction of the map
m
to the subspace
V\otimes_{k}V\subset A\otimes_{k}A

provides a linear map
V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow A=k\oplus V
, whose components
we denote by
m_{V}\colon V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow V
and
m_{k}\colon V\otimes_{k}V\longrightarrow k
.
The restriction of the map
d
to the subspace
V\subset A
is
a linear map
V\longrightarrow A=k\oplus V
, whose components we denote by

d_{V}\colon V\longrightarrow V
and
d_{k}\colon V\longrightarrow k
.

Denote by
\Br(A)
the graded coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(A_{+}[1])
.
(Here, as usual, the cohomological degree shift
[1]
is responsible
for the construction of the total grading on the bigraded vector space

\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{+}^{\otimes n}
.)
One observes that, for any graded
k
-vector space
W
, odd
coderivations
d
of degree
1
on the tensor coalgebra
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)
are
uniquely determined by their corestrictions to
W
, i. e.,
the compositions
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)\longrightarrow W
,
where
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)\longrightarrow W^{\otimes 1}=W
is the direct summand projection.
Moreover, an arbitrary homogeneous linear map
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)\longrightarrow W

of degree
1
corresponds to some odd coderivation
d
on
\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(W)

in this way.

The CDG-coalgebra
\Br^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{v}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=(\Br(A),d,h)
is now
constructed as follows.
The odd coderivation
d\colon\Br(A)\longrightarrow\Br(A)
of (total) degree
1

is defined by the condition that its composition with the projection

\Br(A)\longrightarrow A_{+}
has two possibly nonzero bigrading
components, given by the maps
m_{V}\colon A_{+}\otimes_{k}A_{+}\longrightarrow A_{+}
and

d_{V}\colon A_{+}\longrightarrow A_{+}
.
