below takes acyclic
DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
to acyclic DG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
;
cf. Theorem
9.6
.)
This argument can be found in
[
64
, proof of Theorem 6.4]
,
where it is used to prove coacyclicity rather than acyclicity.

Having dispelled the mystery of “divergent spectral sequences”,
the authors of
[
21
]
embarked upon a program of applying derived
functors (such as Ext and Tor) to DG-modules, and particularly of
defining the derived functor Cotor (of the functor of cotensor product
of comodules) and applying it to DG-comodules
[
23
]
.
The program culminated in the paper
[
34
]
, where is was pointed
out that the two hypercohomology spectral sequences for DG-modules
(as well as, generally speaking, for unbounded complexes) converge, in
one sense or another, to
two different limits
.
These two limits were called the
differential derived functors
of the first
and
second kind
in
[
34
]
.
The only difference between their constructions consisted in using two
different ways of totalize a given bicomplex: taking either direct sums,
or direct products along the diagonals
[
34
, Definition I.4.1]
.
In retrospective, one can say that
classical homological algebra

(as exemplified by
[
15
]
but not
[
21
]
) consisted in
studying complexes under suitable boundedness or finite homological
dimension assumptions guaranteeing that the two kinds of differential
derived functors agree.

7.4.
Philosophy of derived categories of the second kind, II

No derived categories were mentioned in the papers
[
21
,
23
,
34
]
,
but in the subsequent decades people became interested in derived
categories (including derived categories of DG-modules).
An important property of a well-behaved derived category is that it
can be defined in two or more equivalent ways: either as the category
of complexes up to an
equivalence relation
(such as
the quasi-isomorphism), or as a category of
adjusted
complexes
(such as complexes of projective or injective objects), which can be
used as
resolutions
for constructing derived functors.
From the derived category perspective,
classical homological
algebra
is the realm where the equivalence relation on complexes is
the conventional quasi-isomorphism, and the adjustedness conditions
are the usual termwise conditions: the resolutions are the complexes
of injective modules, or the complexes of projective modules, etc.

In particular, for any abelian category
\mathsf{A}
with enough injective
objects, the bounded below derived category
\mathsf{D}^{+}(\mathsf{A})
is equivalent
to the homotopy category of bounded below complexes of injective
objects in
\mathsf{A}
.
Dually, for any abelian category
\mathsf{B}
with enough projectives objects,
the bounded above derived category
\mathsf{D}^{-}(\mathsf{B})
is equivalent to
the homotopy category of bounded above complexes of projective objects
in
\mathsf{B}
.
These are the classical homological algebra settings.

Here is a thematic counterexample showing that the assertions in
the previous paragraph are
not
true for unbounded complexes.
Let
\Lambda=k[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{2})
be the exterior algebra
in one variable (or in other words, the ring of dual numbers).
Consider the unbounded complex of free
\Lambda
-modules with
one generator

(2)

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Lambda\overset{\varepsilon}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}\Lambda\overset{\varepsilon}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}\Lambda\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb,

where all the differentials are the operators of multiplication
with
\varepsilon
.
Then (
2
) is an unbounded
complex of projective (also, injective)
\Lambda
-modules which is
acyclic, but not contractible.
So (
2
) is a zero object
in the derived category
\mathsf{D}(\Lambda{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
, but a nonzero object in
the homogopy category of complexes of projective (or injective)
objects in
\Lambda{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.
The point is that (
2
) is
a quite
nonzero
object in the coderived category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\Lambda{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
.

Denote by
C=\Lambda^{*}
the dual vector space to
\Lambda
with
its natural coalgebra structure.
Then the triangulated equivalence of
Theorem
2.7

essentially (up to obvious grading effects) assigns the acyclic
complex (
2
), viewed
as a complex of
C
-comodules (as per the construction in
Section
6.3
), to the
k[x]
-module

k[x,x^{-1}]
from Example
1.2
(2).

Remark 7.2
.

Now we can present an example of “divergent spectral sequence”
in Koszul duality promised in
Remark
7.1
.
Let
(C,\gamma)
be a (say, conilpotent) coaugmented coalgebra
over
k
.
Similarly to
Remark
4.3
,
we observe that the functor
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

from Theorem
2.7

need not
take acyclic complexes of comodules over
C
to acyclic
DG-modules over the DG-algebra
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
.

Indeed, consider the coalgebra
C=\Lambda^{*}
as above, and
the complex of

C
-comodules (
2
).
Then the DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C)
is, in fact,
the polynomial ring
A=k[x]
, endowed with the obvious grading
and the zero differential.
The functor
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto C\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
takes the graded

A
-module
k[x,x^{-1}]
(viewed as a DG-module with zero
differential) to the acyclic complex of

C
-comodules (
2
).

It is a part of
Theorem
2.7
that
the adjunction morphism
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a quasi-isomorphism of DG-modules for any DG-module

M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
It follows that the functor
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,{-})=A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}

takes the acyclic complex of

C
-comodules (
2
) to
a nonacyclic DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
quasi-isomorphic to
k[x,x^{-1}]
.

How can it happen?
Consider the decreasing filtration
F
on the graded vector
space
\Cb_{\gamma}(C)=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}C^{+}{}^{\otimes n}
defined
by the obvious rule
F^{n}\Cb_{\gamma}(C)=\bigoplus_{i=n}^{\infty}C^{+}{}^{\otimes i}
.
Given a complex of
C
-comodules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, let
F
be the induced
filtration on the graded vector space
\Cb_{\gamma}(C,N)=\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\otimes_{k}N
; so
F^{n}\Cb_{\gamma}(C,N)=F^{n}\Cb_{\gamma}(C)\otimes_{k}N
.
Then
F^{n}\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a subcomplex in

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
for every
n\geq 0
.
Furthermore, the quotient complex
F^{n}\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})/F^{n+1}\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is the tensor product

C^{+}{}^{\otimes n}\otimes_{k}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with the differential induced by
the differential on
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
So all the quotient complexes
F^{n}\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})/F^{n+1}\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are acyclic whenever
a complex of
C
-comodules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic.
Still, the complex
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
need
not
be
acyclic.
How can it happen?

This is what people naïvely call a “divergent spectral sequence”.
In fact, the spectral sequence associated with the filtration
F
on
the cobar-complex
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
does not diverge at all;
it just does not converge to the cohomology of the complex

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
It is not supposed to.
The point is that the decreasing filtration
F
on the complex

\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
not complete
.
Simply put, if one wants the spectral sequence of a decreasing
filtration on the totalization of a bicomplex to converge to
the cohomology of the totalization, one needs to totalize the bicomplex
by taking
direct products
along the diagonals and
not the direct sums.
The spectral sequence associated with the filtration
F
on
the cobar-complex
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
converges (at least in
the weak sense of
[
21
, Theorem 7.4]
) to the cohomology of
the
completion
of the complex
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
,
that is, to the cohomology of the
direct product totalization

of the bicomplex.

Let us denote by
\ovCb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
the completed cobar-complex,
i. e., the direct product totalization of the cobar-bicomplex of
C

with the coefficients in
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then, by
[
21
, Theorem 7.4]
, the complex
\ovCb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(C,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
indeed acyclic for any acyclic complex of
C
-comodules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

(cf. Remark
2.8
).
The completed cobar-construction is an interesting functor on its own;
a discussion of it can be found, e. g.,
in
[
58
, Section 8 of Chapter 5]
.
But it is
not
the adjoint functor to the functor

M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto C\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
Theorem
2.7

(cf. Section
5.4
).

As suggested in the first paragraph of the introduction
to
[
64
]
(see
[
64
, Section 0.1]
),
if one’s
spectral sequence diverges, one should either replace the complex
with its completion, or choose a different filtration
.
In the context of Koszul duality, it is better to choose
a different filtration.
That is what one accomplishes by replacing acyclic complexes or
DG-comodules with coacyclic ones.

The reader can find a further introductory discussion of
the complex (
2
) and its place
in Koszul duality in the book
[
75
, Prologue]
, and an introductory
discussion of derived categories of the second kind largely centered
around the example (
2
)
in the paper
[
78
, Section 5]
.
This material goes back to
[
64
, Example 3.3]
, where another
counterexample can be also found: a totally finite-dimensional
(in fact, two-dimensional) DG-module
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over a (two-dimensional)
DG-algebra
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
such that the underlying graded
B
-module
M
is
projective and injective, while the DG-module
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic
but not contractible.

The triangulated equivalence of
Theorem
5.4

assigns the acyclic DG-module
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
(viewed as a DG-comodule
over the DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{}^{*}
) to the
k[x]
-module
k_{1}

(
a=1
) from Example
1.2
(2).
Both the acyclic complex (
2
)
and the acyclic DG-module
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
can be also viewed as assigned to
the respective modules over the abelian Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=k
by
the triangulated equivalence (
1
)
from Example
2.5
.

Example 7.3
.

To give a further series of examples, let us continue the discussion
of Lie algebras from Example
2.5
.
Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional semisimple Lie algebra over
a field
k
of characteristic
0
, and let
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
be
its cohomological Chevalley–Eilenberg complex.
To any complex of modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
\mathfrak{g}
, the Koszul
duality (
1
) assigns
the cohomological Chevalley–Eilenberg complex (or rather, the total
complex of the bicomplex)
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes_{k}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, with
the differential including summands induced by the Lie bracket
on
\mathfrak{g}
, the action of
\mathfrak{g}
in
M
, and the differential on
M
.
Here the complex
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes_{k}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is viewed a
a DG-module over the DG-algebra
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
.

In particular, for any
\mathfrak{g}
-module
M
, the Chevalley–Eilenberg
complex
(\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes_{k}M,\>d)
computes the Lie algebra
cohomology
H^{*}(\mathfrak{g},M)
with the coefficients in
M
.
Now one has
H^{*}(\mathfrak{g},M)=0
for any nontrivial finite-dimensional
irreducible
\mathfrak{g}
-module
M
.
So
(\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes_{k}M,\>d)
is another example of
a finite-dimensional acyclic, noncontractible DG-module whose
underlying graded module is both projective and injective.

Let us explain in yet another way why a triangulated equivalence
such as (
1
)
cannot
hold
with the conventional derived category
\mathsf{D}(\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

used instead of the coderived category.
Any quasi-isomorphism of DG-algebras
f\colon A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence of the derived categories of DG-modules,

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

[
41
, Example 6.1]
,

[
64
, Theorem 1.7]
.
It is well-known that, for a semisimple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
in

\operatorname{char}k=0
, the DG-algebra
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is
formal (i. e., quasi-isomorphic to its cohomology algebra);
indeed, the subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
-invariant elements in

\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is a DG-subalgebra with zero differential
mapping quasi-isomorphically onto the cohomology.
Moreover, the cohomology algebra
H^{*}(\mathfrak{g})=H^{*}(\mathfrak{g},k)
is not very
informative: it is an exterior (free graded commutative) algebra
with generators in certain odd degrees.
There is
no
hope of recovering the derived category of

\mathfrak{g}
-modules
\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{g}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
from the quasi-isomorphism class of
the DG-algebra
\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
.

So phenomena appearing to be specific to unbounded complexes also
manifest themselves in totally bounded, finite-dimensional DG-modules.
The explanation is that, if one wants to stay within the realm of
classical homological algebra, then one has to restrict oneself to
suitably bounded DG-modules over DG-rings
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
belonging to
one of the following two classes: either
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is nonpositively
cohomologically graded
[
64
, Theorem 3.4.1]
, or
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is connected, simply connected, and positively cohomologically
graded
[
64
, Theorem 3.4.2]
.
The DG-algebra
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
from
[
64
, Example 3.3]
(discussed
above), as well as, more generally, the DG-algebra

\Lambda^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
from Examples
2.5

and
7.3
, belong to neither
of the two classes.

7.5.
Philosophy of derived categories of the second kind, III

Thus the choice between derived categories of the first and the second
kind can be presented as consisting in
having to decide what to do
with the complex (
2
)
.
Is it to be considered as a trivial object and not acceptable as
a resolution (for computing derived functors with it), or as
a nontrivial object and acceptable as a resolution?
We can now summarize the philosophy of two kinds of derived categories
as follows.

In derived categories of the first kind (the conventional derived
categories):

•

a complex or a DG-module is viewed as a deformation of its
cohomology;

•

the conventional quasi-isomorphism (that is, the property of
a morphism to induce an isomorphism on the cohomology objects) is
the equivalence relation on complexes or DG-modules in the derived
category;

•

strong and complicated conditions need to be imposed on
resolutions: the adjusted complexes or DG-modules are known as
the
homotopy projectives
,
homotopy flats
,

homotopy injectives
etc.; in slightly different terminological
systems they are called
K
-projectives
,
K
-flats
,

K
-injectives
etc., or
DG-projectives
,

DG-flats
,
DG-injectives
, etc. (see
[
81
, Remark 6.4]
for the terminological discussion).

It is important here that one
cannot
tell whether a complex or
DG-module is homotopy adjusted (i. e., can be used as a resolution
for derived categories or derived functors of the first kind) by
looking only on the terms of the complex or on the underlying graded
module of a DG-module.
The property of a complex/DG-module to be homotopy adjusted

depends
on the differential.
The classical works where the theory of derived categories of
the first kind was developed are
[
86
,
41
,
29
]
.

In derived categories of the second kind (the coderived,
contraderived, and absolute derived categories):

•

a complex or a (C)DG-module is viewed as a deformation of its
underlying graded module, or of itself endowed with the zero
differential;

•

strong and complicated equivalence relations are imposed on
complexes or DG-modules in the derived categories; the related
classes of trivial objects are called the
coacyclic
,

contraacyclic
, or
absolutely acyclic
complexes or
(C)DG-modules; for conilpotent (C)DG-coalgebras, the equivalence
relation of
filtered quasi-isomorphism
is used;

•

all the conventional termise injective or termwise projective
complexes, or (C)DG-modules with injective/projective underlying
graded modules are adjusted (i. e., can be used as resolutions);
more precisely:

–

in the coderived categories, all complexes of injective
objects or graded-injective (C)DG-modules are acceptable as
resolutions;

–

in the contraderived categories, all complexes of projective
objects or graded-projective (C)DG-modules are acceptable as
resolutions.

It is important here that one
cannot
tell whether a morphism
is a weak equivalence for a derived category of the second kind,
or whether a complex or DG-module is coacyclic/contraacyclic/etc. by looking only on its underlying complex of abelian groups.
The property of a complex/DG-module to be co- or contraacyclic

depends
on the module structure.
The classical works where the theory of derived categories of
the second kind was developed are
[
30
,
47
,
42
,
38
,
46
,
35
,
52
,
44
,
63
,
64
,
19
,
5
,
53
,
87
]
.

The terminology
coderived category
reflects an understanding
that the coderived categories are most suitable for
comodules
.
This was perhaps an intended meaning of the term in Keller’s
note
[
42
]
, where it was first introduced (in fact, in
the original definition in
[
42
]
, the coderived category was
defined for DG-comodules
only
; the definition was not
applicable to modules or DG-modules).

Dually, the
contraderived category
is most suitable for

contramodules
, while the conventional derived category works
best for complexes of modules or DG-modules.
This philosophy was developed and played a central role in the present
author’s monograph on semi-infinite homological algebra
[
63
]
.
The technical aspects of the assertion “coderived categories are
best behaved for comodules, contraderived categories for contramodules,
and conventional derived categories for modules” are discussed in
detail in the memoir
[
64
, Sections 1–4]
(cf. Sections
7.6
–
7.7

and
9.3
below).

But there are also other considerations.
In particular,
the conventional derived category makes no sense
for curved structures
.
There is a partial exception to this rule in the (complicated,
technical, and special)
weakly curved
case, as developed
in
[
65
]
.
But generally speaking, CDG-modules (as well as CDG-rings,
CDG-comodules etc.) have
no
cohomology groups or modules,
so the notion of a conventional quasi-isomorphism is

undefined
for them (as we mentioned already in
Section
6.1
).
Thus, for CDG-modules one has to consider derived categories of
the second kind.

This presents no problem for Koszul duality.
But, say, derived categories of
matrix factorizations
can be
only defined as derived categories of the second kind
[
57
,
59
,
19
]
.
So sometimes one is forced to work with the coderived categories of
modules, technically complicated as they might be.
Conversely, differential derived functors of the first kind for
DG-comodules appear in the context of the Eilenberg–Moore
spectral sequence
[
23
]
; see
[
63
, Section 0.2.10]

for a discussion.

7.6.
Coderived and contraderived categories of CDG-modules

To any bicomplex one can assign its total complex.
In fact, there are several ways to do it: at least, one has to choose
between taking infinite direct sums or infinite products along
the diagonals.
There are further possibilities, which may be called
the
Laurent totalizations
(as in “the Laurent formal
power series”): one can take direct sums in one direction and
direct products in the other one
[
34
, Definition I.3.4]
.
For bicomplexes with only a finite number of nonzero terms on every
diagonal, there is only one way of producing the total complex.

Here we are interested in a special class of
bicomplexes with
three rows
: namely, the
short exact sequences of complexes
.
It is straightforward to see that the total complex of any short exact
sequence of complexes (say, in an abelian category, or even in
an exact category) is acyclic.

The concept of totalization can be extended to DG-categories, where
one can speak of the (product or coproduct) totalization of a complex
of objects and
closed morphisms of degree
0
between
them
[
64
, Section 1.2]
,
[
77
, Section 1.3]
.
In particular, one can consider complexes (e. g., short exact
sequences) of DG-modules or CDG-modules, and their totalizations.

Specifically, let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(B,d)
be a DG-ring.
A
short exact sequence of DG-modules

0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow 0
over
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
consists of three (say, left)
DG-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and two morphisms

f\in\Hom^{0}_{B}(K,L)
and
g\in\Hom^{0}_{B}(L,M)
such that
d(f)=0=d(g)
in
the respective complexes
\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{B}(K,L)
and
\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{B}(L,M)
, and

0\longrightarrow K\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}L\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}M\longrightarrow 0
is
a short exact sequence of graded
B
-modules.
The
totalization

\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
of
0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow 0
is constructed by passing to
the direct sums of the (at most three) grading components along every
diagonal, and setting the total differential to be the sum of
d_{K}
,

d_{L}
,
d_{M}
,
f
, and
g
with suitable signs.
Then
\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is again a (left)
DG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

The definitions of a
short exact sequence of CDG-modules

0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
over a CDG-ring

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and its
total CDG-module

\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are spelled out literally the same (recall that the Hom
of two CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a complex, as explained in
Section
6.2
).
The totalization can be also constructed as an iterated cone: take
the cone
\cone(f)
of the closed morphism
f\colon K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
in
the DG-category of CDG-modules
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, and put

\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=\cone(\cone(f)\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
(We are only speaking of the totalization as defined up to some
cohomological grading shift, and refrain from specifying any preferred
choice of such grading shift, as it is not important for our purposes.)

Let
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
denote the homotopy category of left
CDG-modules over a CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, i. e., the category whose
objects are the CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and morphisms are
closed morphisms of degree
0
up to cochain homotopy.
As the DG-category
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
has shifts and cones, its homotopy
category
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
is a triangulated category
[
11
]
.

Definition 7.4

(
[
64
, Section 3.3]
)
.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-ring.
A left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be
absolutely acyclic

if it belongs to the minimal thick subcategory of
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

containing the totalizations of short exact sequences of
left CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The full subcategory of absolutely acyclic CDG-modules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

is called the
absolute derived category
of left CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Definition 7.5

(
[
63
, Section 2.1]
,
[
64
, Section 3.3]
)
.

A left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be
coacyclic

if it belongs to the minimal triangulated subcategory
of
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
containing the totalizations of short exact
sequences of left CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

and closed under
infinite direct sums
.
The thick subcategory of coacyclic CDG-modules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

is called the
coderived category
of left CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Definition 7.6

(
[
63
, Section 4.1]
,
[
64
, Section 3.3]
)
.

A left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be
contraacyclic

if it belongs to the minimal triangulated subcategory
of
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
containing the totalizations of short exact
sequences of left CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

and closed under
infinite products
.
The thick subcategory of contraacyclic CDG-modules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

is called the
contraderived category
of left CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Example 7.7

(
[
64
, Examples 3.3]
)
.

The doubly unbounded acyclic complex of
\Lambda
-modules in
the formula (
2
)
from Section
7.4

is
neither
coacyclic,
nor
contraacyclic.
Its (bounded above) acyclic subcomplex of canonical truncation
is
contraacyclic
, but
not
coacyclic.
Dually, the (bounded below) acyclic quotient complex of canonical
truncation of (
2
) is

coacyclic
, but
not
contraacyclic.
Consequently,
neither
one of the three complexes is
absolutely acyclic.
We refer to
[
78
, beginning of Section 5]
for a detailed
discussion.

The maximal natural generality for
Definitions
7.4
–
7.6
is that of
exact
DG-categories

[
77
, Section 5.1]
.
This concept, suggested in
[
64
, Section 3.2 and
Remarks 3.5–3.7]
, was worked out in the preprint
[
77
]
.

The following two theorems are the main results of the basic theory
of coderived, contraderived, and absolute derived categories of
CDG-modules.

Theorem 7.8
.

(a)
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring whose underlying graded
ring
B
has finite left global dimension (as a graded ring; i. e.,
the abelian category of graded left
B
-modules has finite homological
dimension).
Then the three classes of coacyclic, contraacyclic, and absolutely
acyclic CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
coincide,

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}),

and accordingly, the three derived categories of the second kind
coincide,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}).

(b)
Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(A,d)
be a DG-ring whose underlying graded
ring
A
has finite left global dimension (as a graded ring).
Assume additionally that
either

A^{n}=0
for all
n>0
,

or otherwise

A^{n}=0
for all
n<0
, the ring
A^{0}
is
(classically) semisimple, and
A^{1}=0
.
Then the three classes of coacyclic, contraacyclic, and absolutely
acyclic DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
coincide with the class of all
acyclic DG-modules,

\mathsf{Ac}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}).

Accordingly, all the four derived categories coincide,

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}).

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
64
, Theorem 3.6(a)]
.
For an alternative proof based on the concept of a
cotorsion
pair
, see
[
76
, Corollaries 4.8 and 4.15]
.
For a generalization to exact DG-categories (covering also
Theorems
7.12

and
9.3
below),
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.6]
, or for a much wider generalization
with weaker assumptions,
[
77
, Theorem 8.9]
.

Part (b) for complexes of modules over a ring of finite global
dimension is a particular case of
[
63
, Remark 2.1]
.
In full generality, part (b) is provable by comparing part (a)
with
[
64
, Theorem 3.4.1(d)]
(in the first case) or
with
[
64
, Theorem 3.4.2(d)]
(in the second case).
∎

For a further discussion of assumptions under which a result like
Theorem
7.8
(b) holds, explore
the references in
[
64
, last paragraph of Section 3.6]
.
In particular, by
[
64
, Theorem 9.4]
, one has

\mathsf{Ac}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
for any
cofibrant
DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

over a commutative ring of finite global dimension.

Let
B
be a graded ring.
The following conditions on
B
are relevant for the theory of derived
categories of the second kind
[
64
, Sections 3.7 and 3.8]
:

(
*
)

any countable direct sum of injective graded left

B
-modules has finite injective dimension (as a graded
B
-module);

(
{*}{*}
)

any countable product of projective graded left

B
-modules has finite projective dimension (as a graded
B
-module).

Notice that any graded ring
B
of finite left global dimension
(as in Theorem
7.8
(a))
satisfies both (
*
) and (
{*}{*}
).

One denotes by
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
the full
triangulated subcategory in the homotopy category formed by all
the CDG-modules
J^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(J,d_{J})
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
whose underlying graded
left
B
-modules
J
are injective (as graded left
B
-modules).
Such CDG-modules
J^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are called

graded-injective
.

Dually, one denotes by
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

the full triangulated subcategory in the homotopy category formed by all
the CDG-modules
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(P,d_{P})
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
whose underlying graded
left
B
-modules
P
are projective.
Such CDG-modules
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are called

graded-projective
.

Theorem 7.9
.

(a)
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring whose underlying
graded ring
B
satisfies condition
(
*
)
.
Then the composition
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
and the Verdier quotient
functor
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
is
an equivalence of triangulated categories,

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}).

(b)
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring whose underlying
graded ring
B
satisfies condition
(
{*}{*}
)
.
Then the composition
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
and the Verdier quotient
functor
\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
is
an equivalence of triangulated categories,

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}).

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
64
, Theorem 3.7]
, and part (b)
is
[
64
, Theorem 3.8]
.
For an alternative proof based on the notion of a
cotorsion
pair
, see
[
76
, Corollary 4.18]
for part (a)
and
[
76
, Corollary 4.9]
for part (b).
For a generalization to exact DG-categories (covering also
Theorems
7.13

and
9.4
below),
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.10]
.
∎

7.7.
Coderived category of CDG-comodules

This section is a comodule version of the previous one.
In the spirit of the discussion in the end of
Section
7.5
, we will see
(in Theorem
7.13
) that
the coderived categories of comodules are somewhat better behaved
than the coderived categories of modules.
On the other hand, it makes
no
sense to consider
“contraderived categories of comodules”, as the functors of
infinite products are usually
not
exact in comodule categories.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Similarly to Section
7.6
,
one can speak of short exact sequences
0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
of left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and their
totalizations (total CDG-comodules)
\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
,
which are again left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Let
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
denote the homotopy category of left
CDG-comodules over a CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Similarly to Section
7.6
,
the DG-category
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
has shifts and cones, so its
homotopy category
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
is triangulated.

Definition 7.10

(
[
64
, Section 4.2]
)
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-coalgebra.
A left CDG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be
absolutely
acyclic
if it belongs to the minimal thick subcategory of

\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
containing the totalizations of short exact
sequences of left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The full subcategory of absolutely acyclic CDG-comodules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})

is called the
absolute derived category
of left CDG-comodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Definition 7.11

(
[
63
, Section 2.1]
,
[
64
, Section 4.2]
)
.

A left CDG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be
coacyclic

if it belongs to the minimal triangulated subcategory
of
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
containing the totalizations of short exact
sequences of left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

and closed under
infinite direct sums
.
The thick subcategory of coacyclic CDG-comodules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})

is called the
coderived category
of left CDG-comodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

In the context of the next theorem, one can keep in mind that,

unlike
for rings, the left and right global dimensions agree
for any (ungraded or graded) coalgebra
C
over a field
k

(see
[
64
, beginning of Section 4.5]
).

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
whose
underlying graded coalgebra
C
has finite left global dimension
(as a graded coalgebra; i. e., the abelian category of graded left

C
-comodules has finite homological dimension).
Then the two classes of coacyclic and absolutely acyclic
CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
coincide,

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}),

and accordingly, the two derived categories of the second kind coincide,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 4.5(a)]
.
For a generalization to exact DG-categories,
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.6(a) or Theorem 8.9(a)]
.
∎

It would be interesting to obtain a DG-comodule version of
Theorem
7.8
(b).
The nonexistence of a meaningful notion of a “contraacyclic
DG-comodule” stands in the way of applying an argument similar to
the proof of Theorem
7.8
(b) above
(cf.
[
64
, Section 4.3]
).
The case of complexes of comodules over a coalgebra of finite global
dimension is covered by
[
63
, Remark 2.1]
.

Notice that the comodule version of condition (
*
) from
Section
7.6
holds for

any
(graded) coalgebra
C
over a field
k
, because the class
of all injective
C
-comodules is closed under infinite direct sums.
Indeed, the injective
C
-comodules are the direct summands of
the cofree ones (cf. Section
2.1
);
and cofree left
C
-comodules have the form
C\otimes_{k}V
, where
V

ranges over (graded)
k
-vector spaces; so cofree
C
-comodules
obviously form a class closed under infinite direct sums.

Similarly to Section
7.6
,
we denote by
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})

the full triangulated subcategory in the homotopy category formed by all
the CDG-comodules whose underlying graded
C
-comodules are injective.
Such CDG-comodules are called
graded-injective
.

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Then the composition
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
of the triangulated
inclusion functor
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})

and the Verdier quotient functor
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
is an equivalence of triangulated categories,

\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 4.4(c)]
.
For a generalization to exact DG-categories,
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.10(a)]
.
∎

Remark 7.14
.

For any DG-ring
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(A,d)
, the derived category of DG-modules

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
is compactly generated (in the sense
of
[
50
,
51
]
).
In fact, it follows immediately from the definitions that the free
DG-module
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a single compact generator of

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

[
43
, Section 3.5]
.
On the coalgebra side of Koszul duality, the related observation is
that, for any CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
over
k
, the coderived
category of CDG-comodules
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})
is compactly
generated.
The CDG-comodules whose underlying graded
k
-vector spaces are
(totally) finite-dimensional form a set of compact generators of

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})

[
64
, Section 5.5]
.
The latter result has many analogous versions and far-reaching
generalizations, including the case of the coderived category of
CDG-modules over a graded Noetherian
CDG-ring
[
64
, Section 3.11]
(see
also
[
19
, Proposition 1.5(d)]
), the Becker coderived category
of a locally coherent abelian category
[
87
, Corollary 6.13]
,
the coderived category of a locally Noetherian abelian
DG-category
[
77
, Theorem 9.23]
, and the coderived category
of a locally coherent abelian DG-category under a certain assumption
of “finite fp-projective dimension”
[
77
, Theorem 9.39]
.

7.8.
History, II, and conclusion

Let me try to explain my original (end of March 1999) motivation
for introducing Definitions
7.5

and
7.11
.
The following remark, purporting to serve as the explanation, is
written in the notation of twisting cochains, because it is very
convenient; though back in Spring 99 I was not familiar with
twisting cochains.
Rather, I was thinking in terms of Koszul duality functors arising
in the context of bar- and cobar-constructions, such as in
Theorems
2.1
,

2.6
,
and
2.7
.

Remark 7.15
.

The trouble with Koszul duality functors is that they can take
acyclic complexes or DG-comodules to nonacyclic ones, as illustrated
by the example in Remark
7.2
.
By contrast, one can notice that the Koszul duality functors
mentioned above in this survey always take coacyclic objects to

contractible
ones!

In full generality, let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-algebra,
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be
a CDG-coalgebra, and
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a twisting cochain.
Then the CDG-module
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
contractible for any coacyclic CDG-comodule
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Similarly, the CDG-comodule
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
contractible for any coacyclic CDG-module
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Indeed, let us consider, e. g., a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
of CDG-comodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Notice that, for any CDG-comodule
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
the underlying graded
B
-module structure of the CDG-module

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
does not depend either on the differential
or on the graded
C
-comodule structure on
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, but only on
the underlying graded vector space of
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
But as a short exact sequence of graded vector spaces, the sequence

0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
is split.
Consequenly, the induced short sequence of CDG-modules

0\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is not only exact, but its
underlying short exact sequence of graded
B
-modules is even

split exact
.

Now one can easily see that, for any short exact sequence of
CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
that is split exact as a short exact
sequence of graded
B
-modules, the corresponding total CDG-module
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contractible.
Obviously, one has
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=\Tot(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
Thus the CDG-module
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contractible.

While my work on derived categories of the second kind remained
unwritten until 2007 or 09, other people were approaching
the same or almost the same concepts from various angles.
Filtered quasi-isomorphisms of DG-coalgebras were introduced
by Hinich
[
30
]
back in 1998, and the same approach was
extended to DG-comodules by K. Lefèvre-Hasegawa
[
47
]

in 2003.
In the same year, the term
coderived category
, together
with its first definition for DG-comodules, appeared in
Keller’s note
[
42
]
.

The study of the homotopy category of unbounded complexes of
projective modules was initiated by Jørgensen
[
38
]
in 2003,
and for the homotopy category of unbounded complexes of injective
objects in a locally Noetherian Grothendieck abelian category this
was done by Krause
[
46
]
in 2004.
Later this line of research was taken up and developed by Neeman
in
[
52
,
53
]
and St​’ovíček in
[
87
]
.

In the meantime, essentially the same constructions
as in my Definitions
7.4
–
7.6
, of what they called
“derived categories” in quotes, were arrived at by
Keller, Lowen, and Nicolás
[
44
]
in 2006, but their results
remained unavailable to the public until after my seminar talk
in Paris in April 2009.
The first
arXiv
version of my research monograph
[
63
]
,
where Definitions
7.5
–
7.6
were spelled out in
the context of exact categories, appeared in 2007; and the first

arXiv
version of the memoir
[
64
]
, where these
definitions were presented and studied for CDG-modules, CDG-comodules,
and CDG-contramodules, became available in 2009.

To summarize, the theory of derived categories of the second kind
identifies three constructions, showing that they produce one and
the same triangulated category.
Here
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
is a conilpotent CDG-coalgebra over a field
k
:

(1)

the coderived category of left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

defined as in the note
[
42
, Section 4]
, using
the cobar-construction in order to pass to DG-modules over
the DG-algebra
\Cb^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\gamma}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
;

(2)

the homotopy category of graded-injective left CDG-comodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(that is, CDG-comodules with injective underlying graded

C
-comodules), which is the CDG-comodule version of the homotopy
category of complexes of injective modules as
in
[
46
, Section 2]
;

(3)

the coderived category of left CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

as per Definition
7.11
, which
is the comodule version of
[
44
, Section 3.1 (A1–A2)]
.

The equivalence of (1) and (3) is a corollary of
Theorem
5.4

(for DG-coalgebras)
or Theorem
6.12

(in full generality).
The equivalence of (2) and (3) does not depend on the conilpotency
assumption and holds for any CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
k
;
this is Theorem
7.13
.

7.9.
Becker’s derived categories of the second kind

From our point of view, the most important development in the theory
of derived categories of the second kind after
[
63
,
64
]
was
Becker’s paper
[
5
]
.
The specific result which is presumed here,

[
5
, Proposition 1.3.6]
, is stated in the language of abelian
model structures; so we will translate it below into the perhaps more
familiar language of triangulated categories.
This is an extension of the approach of
[
38
,
46
,
52
,
53
]

from complexes of modules into the CDG-module realm.

After some hesitation, we dared to put in writing
in
[
81
, Remark 9.2]
the terminology of
derived categories
of the second kind in the sense of Positselski
vs.
derived categories of the second kind in the sense of Becker
.
The definitions in
Sections
7.6
–
7.7
are those of derived categories
of the second kind in my sense.

We are following the expositions in
[
81
, Sections 7 and 9]
(in
the case of abelian categories) and
[
76
, Sections 4.2–4.3]

(in the case case of CDG-modules).
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-ring.
A left CDG-module
X^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be

contraacyclic in the sense of Becker
if the complex of abelian
groups
\Hom_{B}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},X^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is acyclic for any graded-projective
left CDG-module
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The thick subcategory of Becker-contraacyclic CDG-modules in
the homotopy category is denoted by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
; and the related triangulated Verdier quotient
category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

is called the
contraderived category of left CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
in the sense of Becker
.

Dually, a left CDG-module
Y^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be

coacyclic in the sense of Becker
if the complex of abelian
groups
\Hom_{B}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(Y^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},J^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is acyclic for any graded-injective
left CDG-module
J^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The thick subcategory of Becker-coacyclic CDG-modules in
the homotopy category is denoted by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
; and the related triangulated Verdier quotient
category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

is called the
coderived category of left CDG-modules over

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
in the sense of Becker
.

The result of
[
64
, Theorem 3.5]
tells that

co/contraacyclicity in the sense of Positselski implies
co/contraacyclicity in the sense of Becker
.
Thus Becker’s co/contraderived categories are “nonstrictly smaller”
than mine.

Becker’s result
[
5
, Proposition 1.3.6]
tells (or rather,
implies) that the compositions of triangulated functors

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

and

\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

are triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{Hot}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})

for any CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Thus one can say that “the Becker analogue of
Theorem
7.9
holds for any CDG-ring”.

On the other hand, with
[
5
, Proposition 1.3.6]
in mind, one
can interpret Theorem
7.9
as telling
that
under the assumption of condition
(
*
)

or
(
{*}{*}
)
, Becker’s and Positselski’s derived categories
of the second kind agree
.
It is an
open problem
whether they agree for an arbitrary
CDG-ring; in fact, this is not known even for complexes of modules
over a ring (see
[
70
, Examples 2.5(3) and 2.6(3)]
for
a discussion).

Positselski’s and Becker’s derived categories of the second kind
are known to agree for CDG-comodules or CDG-contramodules over
a CDG-coalgebra over a field (see
Theorem
7.13
above
and Theorem
9.4
below).
It is in this sense that one says that “the coderived categories
are (known to be) better behaved for comodules, and the contraderived
categories for contramodules”.

The definitions in
[
44
,
63
,
64
,
59
,
19
,
70
,
78
,
75
,
77
]

are those of co/contraderived categories in the sense of
Positselski.
The definitions in
[
38
,
46
,
52
,
5
,
53
,
87
,
76
,
81
]

are those of co/contraderived categories in the sense of Becker.

8.
Contramodules over Coalgebras

Contramodules are dual analogues of comodules.
To any coalgebra (over a field), one can assign the (abelian)
categories of left and right contramodules over it, alongside with
the comodule categories.
The structure theory of contramodules over a coalgebra over a field
is more complicated, but not too much more complicated, than
the structure theory of comodules.
The historical obscurity/neglect of contramodules is responsible for
the popular misconception that injective objects are much more common
than projective objects in “naturally appearing” abelian categories.
We suggest the survey paper
[
71
]
as the standard reference
source on contramodules.

8.1.
The basics of contramodules

The definitions of coalgebras and comodules (as in
Section
2.1
) are obtained by
writing down the definitions of algebras and modules in the tensor
notation and inverting the arrows.
In order to arrive to the definition of a contramodule, it remains
to notice that there are
two
equivalent ways to express
the definition of a module in a tensor (or Hom) notation.

An algebra over a field
k
is a vector space
A
endowed with
linear maps
m\colon A\otimes_{k}A\longrightarrow A
(the multiplication map) and

e\colon k\longrightarrow A
(the unit map).
A left
A
-module
M
is a
k
-vector space endowed with a linear
map
n\colon A\otimes_{k}M\longrightarrow M
(the action map).
The usual associativity and unitality axioms need to be imposed.
But there is a different way to spell out the definition of a module.

A left
A
-module
M
is the same thing as a
k
-vector space
endowed with a
k
-linear map
p\colon M\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(A,M)
.
This is just an expression of the tensor-Hom adjunction: the maps
n

and
p
are connected by the rule
p(x)(a)=n(a\otimes x)\in M
for all

x\in M
and
a\in A
.
We leave it to the reader to write down the associativity and
unitality axioms for an
A
-module
M
in terms of the map
p
.

The definition of a
contramodule
over a coalgebra
C
over
a field
k
is obtained by inverting the arrows in the definition of
a module given in terms of the map
p
.
We refer to
[
9
, Section 2.6]
for a relevant discussion of
monad-comonad and comonad-monad adjoint pairs.

Specifically, let
C
be a (coassociative and counital) coalgebra
over a field
k
.
A
left
C
-contramodule

P
is a
k
-vector space endowed
with a
k
-linear map of
left
C
-contraaction

\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,P)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muP

satisfying the following
contraassociativity
and

contraunitality
axioms.
Firstly, the two compositions

\Hom_{k}(C,\Hom_{k}(C,P))\simeq\Hom_{k}(C\otimes_{k}C,\>P)\rightrightarrows\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P

must be equal to each other,
\pi\circ\Hom_{k}(C,\pi)=\pi\circ\Hom_{k}(\mu,P)
.
Secondly, the composition

P\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P

must be equal to the identity map,
\pi\circ\Hom_{k}(\epsilon,P)=\mathrm{id}_{P}

[
71
, Section 1.1]
.
Here, as in Section
2.1
,

\mu\colon C\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}C
and
\epsilon\colon C\longrightarrow k
are
the comultiplication and counit maps of the coalgebra
C
.

In the definition of a
left
contramodule, the identification

\Hom_{k}(C,\Hom_{k}(C,P))\simeq\Hom_{k}(C\otimes_{k}C,\>P)
is obtained as
a particular case of the adjunction isomorphism
\Hom_{k}(U,\Hom_{k}(V,W))\simeq\Hom_{k}(V\otimes_{k}U,\>W)
, where
U
,
V
, and
W
are arbitrary

k
-vector spaces.
In the definition of a
right
contramodule, the isomorphism

\Hom_{k}(V,\Hom_{k}(U,W))\simeq\Hom_{k}(V\otimes_{k}U,\>W)
is presumed.

For any right
C
-comodule
N
and any
k
-vector space
V
,
the vector space
\Hom_{k}(N,V)
has a natural structure of left

C
-contramodule (see
[
71
, Section 1.2]
for the details).
The left
C
-contramodule
\Hom_{k}(C,V)
is called the
free

left
C
-contramodule generated by the vector space
V
.

For any left
C
-contramodule
Q
, the
k
-vector space of all
left
C
-contramodule maps
\Hom_{k}(C,V)\longrightarrow Q
is naturally
isomorphic to the
k
-vector space of all
k
-linear maps

V\longrightarrow Q
,

\Hom^{C}(\Hom_{k}(C,V),Q)\simeq\Hom_{k}(V,Q).

Hence the “free contramodule” terminology.

8.2.
Duality-analogy of comodules and contramodules

The duality-analogy (or “covariant duality”) between comodules and
contramodules is a remarkable yet unfamiliar phenomenon.
It can be expressed by saying that the categories of comodules and
contramodules look as though they were opposite categories—up to
a point.
In fact,
no
category of contramodules is ever the opposite
category to a category of comodules; for example, over the coalgebra

C=k
, both the categories of
C
-comodules and
C
-contramodules
coincide with the category of
k
-vector spaces (which is certainly

not
equivalent to its opposite category).
But the
analogy
is striking.

Let
C
be a coalgebra over
k
.
Then
both
the category of left
C
-comodules
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
and
the category of left
C
-contramodules
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
are abelian.

The abelian category
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
has enough injective objects;
in fact, the injective comodules are precisely the direct summands of
the cofree comodules (as defined in
Section
2.1
).
The abelian category
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
has enough projective objects;
in fact, the projective contramodules are precisely the direct summands
of the free contramodules (as defined in
Section
8.1
)

[
71
, Section 1.2]
.

The forgetful functor
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
from the category of

C
-comodules to the category of
k
-vector spaces is exact and
preserves infinite coproducts (but
not
infinite products).
Consequently, the coproduct functors in
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
are exact
(moreover, so are the functors of filtered colimit).

The forgetful functor
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
is exact and preserves
infinite products (but
not
infinite coproducts).
Consequently, the product functors in
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
are exact (but
the filtered limits are
not
exact in
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
, of course,
as they are not exact already in
k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
).

A wide class of abelian categories to which the categories of comodules
belong is called the class of
Grothendieck categories
.
A wide class of abelian categories to which the categories of
contramodules belong is called the class of
locally presentable
abelian categories with enough projective objects
.
The main reference source on the latter class of abelian categories
is the preprint
[
72
]
(see also
[
79
, Section 6]
).
The duality-analogy (or “covariant duality”) between these two
classes of abelian categories is emphasized in the paper
[
81
]

(cf.
[
79
,
80
]
).

8.3.
Comodules and contramodules over power series in
one variable

The aim of this section is to introduce the intuition of contramodules
as
modules with infinite summation operations
.

The dual vector space to an infinite-dimensional (discrete) vector
space comes endowed with a natural topology; such topological vector
spaces are known as
linearly compact
or
pseudocompact

(or
pro-finite-dimensional
).
In particular, the dual vector spaces to infinite-dimensional
coalgebras are topological algebras.
In fact, the category of coalgebras is anti-equivalent to the category
of linearly compact topological algebras (see the discussion
in
[
71
, first paragraph of Section 1.3]
).

So coalgebras can be identified by the names of their dual topological
algebras.
In particular, let
kz^{*}
be a one-dimensional vector space with
the basis vector
z^{*}
.
Consider the tensor coalgebra
C=\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(kz^{*})
, as defined in
Section
2.3
.
Then the dual topological algebra to
C
is the algebra
C^{*}=k[[z]]

of formal Taylor power series in the variable
z
(cf. Remark
6.4
).

Let
C
be a coalgebra over
k
.
As explained in Section
6.3
, any comodule
over a coalgebra
C
is a module over the algebra
C^{*}
.
Similarly, any contramodule over
C
is also a module over
C^{*}
.
The composition

C^{*}\otimes_{k}P\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{k}(C,P)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muP

of the natural injective map
C^{*}\otimes_{k}P\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(C,P)
with
the contraaction map
\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P
defines an action
of
C^{*}
in
P
.

We would like to describe comodules and contramodules over
the coalgebra
C=\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(kz^{*})
.
Namely, a
C
-comodule
M
is the same thing as a
k[z]
-module with
a
locally nilpotent
action of the operator
z
.
This means that for every element
m\in M
there exists an integer

n\geq 1
such that
z^{n}m=0
in
M
.
The coaction map
M\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}M
is given in terms of
the
k[z]
-module structure on
M
by the formula

m\longmapsto\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{*}{}^{\otimes n}\otimes z^{n}m\qquad\text{for all $m\in M$},

and the condition of local nilpotence of the action of
z
in
M

comes from the condition that the sum in the right-hand side must be
finite (if it is to define an element of the tensor product
C\otimes_{k}M
).

A
C
-contramodule
P
is the same thing as a
k
-vector space with
the following
z
-power infinite summation operation
.
To every sequence of elements
p_{0}
,
p_{1}
,
p_{2}
…
\in P
,
an element denoted formally by
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}p_{n}\in P
is
assigned.
The following axioms must be satisfied:

\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}(ap_{n}+bq_{n})=a\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}p_{n}+b\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}q_{n}\quad\text{for all $p_{n}$, $q_{n}\in P$, \ $a$, $b\in k$}

(
linearity
),

\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}p_{n}=p_{0}\quad\text{if $p_{1}=p_{2}=\dotsb=0$ in $P$}

(
contraunitality
), and

\sum\nolimits_{i=0}^{\infty}z^{i}\sum\nolimits_{j=0}^{\infty}z^{j}p_{ij}=\sum\nolimits_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}\sum\nolimits_{i,j\geq 0}^{i+j=n}p_{ij}

for all
p_{ij}\in P
(
contraassociativity
).
In the latter equation, the first three summation signs denote
the
z
-power infinite summation operation, while the forth one means
a finite sum of elements in
P

[
71
, Section 1.3]
.

The intuition of contramodules as modules with infinite summation
operations is axiomatized in the concepts of contramodules over
a commutative ring with a finitely generated
ideal
[
68
, Sections 3–4]
,
[
74
, Section 1]
,
contramodules over topological rings and topological associative
algebras
[
71
, Sections 2.1 and 2.3]
,
[
79
, Section 6]
,
contramodules over topological Lie algebras
[
71
, Sections 1.7
and 2.4]
, etc.

One special property of the coalgebra
C=\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(kz^{*})
is that
a
C
-contramodule structure can be uniquely recovered from
its underlying
k[[z]]
-module structure.
In fact, the forgetful functor to the category of modules over
the polynomial algebra
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow k[z]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is already fully
faithful
[
63
, Remark A.1.1]
,
[
68
, Theorem 3.3]
,

[
65
, Theorem B.1.1]
.
This is
not
true for an arbitrary coalgebra
C
, of course;
still this is true for any
finitely cogenerated
conilpotent
coalgebra
C

[
69
, Theorem 2.1]
.
See
[
71
, Section 3.8]
for a discussion of far-reaching
generalizations.

8.4.
Nonseparated contramodules

The duality-analogy between comodules and contramodules has some
limitations, though.
The examples of
nonseparated contramodules
demonstrate
such limitations.

Let
f\colon C\longrightarrow D
be a homomorphism of coalgebras.
Then, as mentioned in
Section
3.1
, every

C
-comodule acquires a
D
-comodule structure.
Similarly, every
C
-contramodule acquires a
D
-contramodule
structure.
The resulting functor
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow D{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
is called
the
contrarestriction of scalars
with respect to
f
.

In particular, let
E\subset C
be a subcoalgebra.
Then the corestriction and contrarestriction of scalars are
fully faithful functors

\displaystyle E{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}},

\displaystyle E{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}.

The functor
E{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
has a right adjoint functor
assigning to every left
C
-comodule
M
its maximal subcomodule

{}_{E}M\subset M
whose
C
-comodule structure comes from
an
E
-comodule structure.
The
E
-comodule
{}_{E}M
can be computed as the kernel of
the composition of maps
M\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}M\longrightarrow C/E\otimes_{k}M
.

The functor
E{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
has a left adjoint functor
assigning to every left
C
-contramodule
P
its maximal
quotient contramodule
{}^{E}\!P
whose
C
-contramodule structure
comes from an
E
-contramodule structure.
The
E
-contramodule
{}^{E}\!P
can be computed as the cokernel of
the composition of maps
\Hom_{k}(C/E,P)\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P
.

According to Lemma
3.1
, any

C
-comodule
M
is the union of its finite-dimensional subcomodules,
and each of these is a comodule over a finite-dimensional subcoalgebra
in
C
.
Therefore, one has

M=\bigcup\nolimits_{E\subset C}{}_{E}M,

where the union is taken over all the finite-dimensional subcoalgebras

E\subset C
.

The dual assertion for contramodules in
not
true.
Specifically, for any
C
-contramodule
P
one can consider
the natural map

(3)

P\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\varprojlim\nolimits_{E\subset C}{}^{E}\!P,

where the inverse limit is taken over all the finite-dimensional
subcoalgebras
E\subset C
.
The map (
3
) is
not
injective
in general.

In fact, there exists an infinite-dimensional coalgebra
C
and
a
two-dimensional

C
-contramodule
P
for which

\varprojlim_{E\subset C}{}^{E}\!P
is a one-dimensional

C
-contramodule.
So the
C
-contramodule structure on
P
does
not
come from
a contramodule structure over any finite-dimensional subcoalgebra
in
C
, and the intersection of the kernels of the maps
P\longrightarrow{}^{E}\!P
is a one-dimensional subcontramodule in the two-dimensional
contramodule
P

[
63
, Section A.1.2]
.

Let us now consider the example of the coalgebra
C=\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$T$}(kz^{*})
from
Section
8.3
.
Then the map (
3
) is simply the
natural map from
P
to the
z
-adic completion of
P
,

(4)

P\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}P/z^{n}P.

The map (
4
) is known to be

surjective
for any
C
-contramodule
P

[
63
, Lemma A.2.3]
,
[
68
, Theorems 3.3 and 5.6]
,

[
74
, Section 1]
.
But there exist
C
-contramodules
P
for which
the map (
4
) is
not injective
.
In other words,
any
C
-contramodule is
z
-adically
complete, but it need not be
z
