-adically separated
.
The now-classical counterexample can be found
in
[
85
, Example 2.5]
,
[
63
, Section A.1.1]
,

[
90
, Example 3.20]
,
[
68
, Example 2.7(1)]
,

[
71
, Section 1.5]
.

In fact, the mentioned counterexample from
[
85
,
63
,
90
]

is a
C
-contramodule
P
with the following property.
There exists a sequence of elements
p_{0}
,
p_{1}
,
p_{2}
, …
\in P

such that
z^{n}p_{n}=0
for every
n\geq 0
, but

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}p_{n}\neq 0
in
P
.
Here we use the
z
-power infinite summation operation notation
from Section
8.3
.
So
the contramodule infinite summation operations cannot be
interpreted as any kind of limits of finite partial
sums

[
63
, Preface]
,

[
71
, Section 0.2 of the Introduction]
.

8.5.
Contramodule Nakayama lemma and irreducible contramodules

As explained in Section
8.4
,
the map (
3
) need not be injective.
However, this map
never
vanishes (for a nonzero

C
-contramodule
P
).

Dropping the contraunitality axiom from the definition of
a contramodule in Section
8.1
, one
obtains the definition of a contramodule over a noncounital
coalgebra
D
.
The following result is called the
contramodule Nakayama lemma

(for contramodules over coalgebras over a field).

Lemma 8.1
.

(a)
Let
D
be a conilpotent noncounital coalgebra
(as defined in Section
3.3
) and
P

be a nonzero
D
-contramodule.
Then the contraaction map
\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(D,P)\longrightarrow P
is
not

surjective.

(b)
Let
C
be a (counital) coalgebra and
P
be a nonzero
left
C
-contramodule.
Then there exists a finite-dimensional subcoalgebra
E\subset C

such that
{}^{E}\!P\neq 0
.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
63
, Lemma A.2.1]
(see
[
65
, Lemma 1.3.1]
,

[
71
, Lemmas 2.1 and 3.22]
and the references therein
for generalizations).
To deduce part (b) from part (a), one has to use the structure theory
of coalgebras, or more specifically, the fact that the maximal
cosemisimple subcoalgebra
C^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset C
is a direct sum of
finite-dimensional (cosimple) coalgebras and the quotient coalgebra
without counit
D=C/C^{\mathrm{ss}}
is conilpotent
(see Section
3.4
)
together with a description of contramodules over an infinite direct
sum of coalgebras
[
63
, Lemma A.2.2]
.
∎

It follows from Lemma
8.1
(b) that any

irreducible

C
-contramodule is finite-dimensional (moreover,
it is a contramodule over a finite-dimensional subcoalgebra in
C
).

In fact, while there is, of course, no way to define a contramodule
structure on an arbitrary
C
-comodule, there does exist a natural
way to define a left
C
-contramodule structure on any

finite-dimensional
left

C
-comodule
[
63
, Section A.1.2]
.
Indeed, the functor
N\longmapsto N^{*}
is an anti-equivalence between
the categories of finite-dimensional right and left
C
-comodules,
and on the other hand, the dual vector space to a right
C
-comodule
is a left
C
-contramodule (as mentioned in
Section
8.1
).
This produces a fully faithful covariant functor from the category of
finite-dimensional left
C
-comodules to the category of
finite-dimensional left
C
-contramodules; a finite-dimensional left

C
-contramodule
P
belongs to the essential image of this functor
if and only if its contramodule structure comes from
an
E
-contramodule structure for some finite-dimensional subcoalgebra

E\subset C
(that is,
P={}^{E}\!P
).

This covariant fully faithful functor restricts to a bijection between
the isomorphism classes of irreducible left
C
-comodules and
irreducible left
C
-contramodules.
Both the sets of isomorphism classes of irreducibles are also naturally
bijective to the set of all cosimple subcoalgebras in
C
, of course.

8.6.
Contratensor product

The formalisms of tensor product and Hom-type operations play a key
role in the book
[
63
]
and the memoir
[
65
]
.
In the ring and module theory, the formalism of tensor/Hom operations
has only two such operations, viz., the tensor product and Hom of
(bi)modules.
In the context of coalgebras, contramodules, etc., the tensor/Hom
operations are more numerous.

In particular, the definition of the
cotensor product
of
comodules goes back, at least, to the paper
[
23
]
.
On the other hand, the construction of the
contratensor product

of a comodule and a contramodule seems to have first appeared
in
[
62
,
63
]
.

Let
C
be a coalgebra,
N
be a right
C
-comodule, and
P
be a left

C
-contramodule.
The
contratensor product

N\odot_{C}P
is a
k
-vector space
constructed as the cokernel of the difference of two natural maps

N\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}(C,P)\rightrightarrows N\otimes_{k}P.

Here the first map
N\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow N\otimes_{k}P
is induced by
the contraaction map
\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P
, while the second
map is the composition
N\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow N\otimes_{k}C\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow N\otimes_{k}P
of the map induced by the coaction map

\nu\colon N\longrightarrow N\otimes_{k}C
and the map induced by the natural evaluation
map
C\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P

[
71
, Section 3.1]
.

For any right
C
-comodule
N
and any
k
-vector space
V
,
there is a natural isomorphism of vector spaces

N\odot_{C}\Hom_{k}(C,V)\simeq N\otimes_{k}V.

For any right
C
-comodule
N
, any left
C
-contramodule
P
, and
any vector space
V
, there is a natural isomorphism of vector spaces

\Hom^{C}(P,\Hom_{k}(N,V))\simeq\Hom_{k}(N\odot_{C}P,\>V),

where, as in Section
8.1
,

\Hom^{C}
denotes the space of morphisms in the category of
left
C
-contramodules.

For a discussion of a generalization of the construction of
contratensor product to topological rings, see
[
79
, Section 7.2]

(some further discussion and references can be found
in
[
71
, end of Section 3.3]
).

8.7.
Underived co-contra correspondence

Let
C
be a (coassociative, counital) coalgebra over a field
k
.
As mentioned in Section
8.2
, there are
enough injective objects in the abelian category of left
C
-comodules,
and these injectives are precisely the direct summands of the cofree
left
C
-comodules
C\otimes_{k}V
(where
V
ranges over the
k
-vector
spaces).
Similarly, there are enough projective objects in the abelian
category of left
C
-contramodules, and these projectives are precisely
the direct summands of the free left
C
-contramodules
\Hom_{k}(C,V)
.

It turns out that there is a natural equivalence between the additive
categories of injective left
C
-comodules and projective left

C
-contramodules,

(5)

C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}.

The simplest way to construct the category
equivalence (
5
) is to define it on
cofree comodules and free contramodules by the rule

C\otimes_{k}V\longleftrightarrow\Hom_{k}(C,V)
.
Then one has to compute that the groups of morphisms agree,

\Hom_{C}(C\otimes_{k}U,\>C\otimes_{k}V)\simeq\Hom_{k}(C\otimes_{k}U,\>V)\\
\simeq\Hom_{k}(U,\Hom_{k}(C,V))\simeq\Hom^{C}(\Hom_{k}(C,U),\,\Hom_{k}(C,V))

for any
k
-vector spaces
U
and
V
, in view of the descriptions
of morphisms into a cofree comodule and from a free contramodule
in Sections
2.1

and
8.1
.
A category-theoretic version of this argument (an equivalence of
Kleisli categories for adjoint comonad-monad pairs) is discussed
in
[
9
, Section 2.6(2)]
.

Another way to obtain the additive category
equivalence (
5
) is to construct
a pair of adjoint functors

(6)

\Hom_{C}(C,{-})\colon C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\leftrightarrows C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\,:\!C\odot_{C}{-}

and check that it restricts to an equivalence between the full
subcategories of injective comodules and projective contramodules.

Here the right adjoint functor
\Hom_{C}(C,{-})
is the Hom functor
in the comodule category
C{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
.
Taking the Hom eats up the left
C
-comodule structure on
C
, but
the right
C
-comodule structure on
C
stays and induces a left

C
-contramodule structure on the Hom space, essentially as
explained in Section
8.1
.
The left adjoint functor
C\odot_{C}{-}
is the functor of contratensor
product.
Taking the contratensor product consumes the right
C
-comodule
structure on
C
, but the left
C
-comodule structure on
C
remains
and induces a left
C
-comodule structure on the contratensor
product (cf.
[
71
, Sections 1.2 and 3.1]
).

For much more advanced discussions of the philosophy of
comodule-contramodule correspondence, see the introductions to
the papers
[
66
,
70
]
.
One important early work related to the co-contra correspondence
is
[
35
, Section 4]
.
From the contemporary point of view, the co-contra correspondence
can be thought of as a particular case of the
tilting-cotilting
correspondence

[
79
,
80
]
.
A discussion of underived versions of the co-contra correspondence
containing various generalizations of the results above in this section
can be found in
[
71
, Sections 3.4–3.6]
.

8.8.
History of contramodules

The notion of a contramodule (over a coalgebra over a commutative
ring) was invented by Eilenberg and Moore and defined
on par

with comodules in the 1965 memoir
[
22
, Section III.5]
.
Besides
[
22
]
, two other papers on contramodules were published
in 1965 and 1970, among them the rather remarkable paper
[
3
]
.
Then contramodules were all but forgotten for 30 to 40 years.

In the meantime, what came to be known as “Ext-
p
-complete” or
“weakly-
l
-complete” abelian groups
[
12
,
36
]
or (in a later
terminology) “cohomologically
I
-adically complete” or
“derived
I
-adically complete modules” (for a finitely generated
ideal
I
in a commutative ring)
[
60
]
were defined and studied
by authors who remained apparently completely unaware of
the connection with Eilenberg and Moore’s contramodules.
We refer to the presentation
[
73
]
for a detailed discussion
of this part of the history.

As described in Section
7.2
,
in Spring 1999 the present author went to the IAS library to look
for prior literature relevant to my March–April 99 discovery of
derived categories of the second kind and their role in derived
nonhomogeneous Koszul duality.
Among other things, I found the definition of a contramodule in
a paper copy of the memoir
[
22
]
which the IAS library had.

In retrospect, I could have realized already then that contramodules
form an ideal context for my definition of the contraderived category
(which I already had in Spring 99).
But I didn’t realize that, though I was a bit unhappy about
the definition of the contraderived category (called “the derived
category
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}
” at the time) having too little use.
The definition of a contramodule felt strange.
As many people had before and would later, I remained unimpressed by
the definition of a contramodule in 1999, though I kept a recollection
of it at the bottom of my memory.

My view of contramodules changed in Summer 2000 when I was working
on the first (2000) part of the “Summer letters on semi-infinite
homological algebra”
[
62
]
.
It was then that I recalled the definition of a contramodule and
realized that contramodules are suited for use as the coefficients
for semi-infinite
cohomology
(as opposed to semi-infinite

homology
) theories.
The concept of comodule-contramodule correspondence was also arrived
at in 2000–02 and reflected in
[
62
]
.
What is now called the contraderived category of contramodules
(together with its mixed counterpart, “the semicontraderived category
of semicontramodules”) was first considered in
[
62
]
.

The realization that derived nonhomogeneous Koszul duality has
a contramodule side, and should be formulated in the form of
a “triality” with the comodule side, the contramodule side,
and the co-contra correspondence forming three sides of a triangular
diagram of triangulated category equivalences, came to me by
mid-’00s.

The letters
[
62
]
were eventually posted to the internet, where
some people could read them, including in particular T. Brzeziński
(as transliterated Russian is somewhat similar to Polish).
This influenced his thinking, as exemplified by publications
such as
[
9
]
.
My own account of contramodule theory first appeared in the August
2007 preprint version of the book
[
63
]
and subsequenly in
the memoir
[
64
]
.

The September 2007 presentation of Brzeziński on
contramodules
[
13
]
featured the following statistics of
MathSciNet search hits:

•

comodules = 797;

•

contramodules = 3.

As I am typing these lines (on July 19, 2022), the current statistics
of MathSciNet search hits (“Anywhere=(
{-}
)”) is

•

comodules = 1457;

•

contramodules = 30.

From our point of view, contramodules (over coalgebras and corings)
should be treated on par and in parallel with comodules
in most contexts.

9.
CDG-Contramodules

The contramodule side of the derived Koszul duality complements
the comodule side.
Together with the derived comodule-contramodule correspondence,
they form a commutative triangle diagram of triangulated category
equivalences called the
Koszul triality
.

9.1.
Graded contramodules

Let
C=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{n}
be a graded coalgebra.
The definition of a
graded
C
-contramodule
is easily
formulated
by analogy
with the definition of an ungraded
contramodule over an ungraded coalgebra in
Section
8.1
.
All one needs to do is to replace the usual ungraded notions of
the tensor product and Hom spaces with the ones intrinsic to the world
of graded vector spaces.

So a
graded left
C
-contramodule
is a graded
k
-vector
space
P
endowed with a
left contraaction map

\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P
, where
\Hom_{k}(C,P)
denotes the graded
Hom space (of homogeneous
k
-linear maps
C\longrightarrow P
of various
degrees
n\in\mathbb{Z}
) and
\pi
is a morphism of graded vector spaces
(i. e., a homogeneous linear map of degree
0
).
The same contraassociativity and contraunitality axioms as in
Section
8.1
are imposed.

However, one can easily get confused trying to define what it means
to
specify a grading
on a given ungraded
C
-contramodule.
The problem is that the conventional thinking of a grading as a direct
sum decomposition that can be either ignored or taken into account at
one’s will is insufficient.
Rather, one needs to think of the
category
of graded vector
spaces and the forgetful functor from it to the category of
ungraded ones.
The point is that there is
more than one
such forgetful functor.

Two
of them are important for the contramodule theory.

A suitable formalism was suggested in
[
63
, Section 11.1.1]
and
then again in
[
75
, Section 2.1]
.
To a graded vector space
V
one can assign
two
ungraded
vector spaces, viz.,
\Sigma V=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{n}
and

\Pi V=\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{n}
.
Further possibilities include the
Laurent summations

\bigoplus_{n<0}V^{n}\oplus\prod_{n\geq 0}V^{n}
and

\prod_{n\leq 0}V^{n}\oplus\bigoplus_{n>0}V^{n}
(cf. the beginning of
Section
7.6
), but for our
purposes we do not need these options.

Given a graded module (or even a nonpositively or nonnegatively graded
ring), one can
choose
between producing its underlying ungraded
module (respectively, ring) by taking the direct sum
or

the direct product of the grading components, that is the forgetful
functor
\Sigma
or
\Pi
.
For coalgebras, comodules, and contramodules, there is no choice.
The underlying ungraded coalgebra or comodule is constructed by
applying the direct sum forgetful functor
\Sigma
.
The underlying ungraded contramodule is obtained by applying
the direct product forgetful functor
\Pi
.

The reason for such preferences in respect to the functors of
forgetting the grading lies in the natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Sigma(U\otimes_{k}V)\simeq\Sigma U\otimes_{k}\Sigma V,

\displaystyle\Pi\Hom_{k}(V,W)\simeq\Hom_{k}(\Sigma V,\Pi W),

which hold for all graded vector spaces
U
,
V
, and
W
.
Here the tensor product/Hom spaces in the left-hand sides of
the formulas are taken in the realm of graded vector spaces, while
in the right-hand sides of the formulas the tensor product/Hom
functors are applied to ungraded vector spaces.

So, if
C
is a graded coalgebra,
M
is a graded
C
-comodule, and

P
is a graded
C
-contramodule, then
\Sigma M
is an ungraded

\Sigma C
-comodule, while
\Pi P
is an ungraded

\Sigma C
-contramodule.
Thus specifying a grading on an ungraded
C
-contramodule
Q
means
decomposing
Q
into a
direct product
of its grading
components
[
64
, Remark 2.2]
.
We refer to
[
64
, Section and Remark 2.2]
for a discussion
of the related sign rules.

9.2.
Definition of CDG-contramodules

Given a DG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, one can spell out the notion of
a (
left
)
DG-contramodule

P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
similarly
to the definition of a DG-comodule in
Section
2.2
.
All one needs to do is to transfer the definition of a contramodule
from Section
8.1
to the world of
complexes of vector spaces.
We skip the details, which can be found in
[
64
, Section 2.3]
,
and pass to the more general case of CDG-contramodules.

Continuing the exposition from Section
6.3
,
we use the language of precomplexes of vector spaces.
Given two precomplexes
V^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(V,d_{V})
and
W^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(W,d_{W})
,
the
Hom precomplex

\Hom^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{k}(V^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},W^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is defined as
explained in Section
6.8
.

Now we can define the notion of a
contraderivation
of
a contramodule.
Let
C
be a graded coalgebra endowed with an odd coderivation

d\colon C\longrightarrow C
of degree
1
, as defined in
Section
6.3
; and let
P
be a graded
left
C
-contramodule.
Then an
odd contraderivation
on
P

compatible with

the coderivation
d
on
C
is a
k
-linear map
d_{P}\colon P\longrightarrow P

of degree
1
such that the left contraaction map

\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,P)\longrightarrow P
is a morphism of precomplexes.
Here the differential
d
on
\Hom_{k}(C,P)
is defined by the rule
from Section
6.8
in terms of
the differentials
d
on
C
and
d_{P}
on
P
.

Furthermore, as explained in
Section
8.3
, any contramodule

P
over a coalgebra
C
is naturally a module over the algebra
C^{*}
.
This construction has an obvious graded version: any graded
contramodule
P
over a graded coalgebra
C
is naturally endowed
with a graded module structure over the graded dual vector space
C^{*}

to
C
, with the natural structure of graded algebra on
C^{*}
.
Similarly to the discussion in Section
6.3
,
one has to choose between two opposite ways of defining
the multiplication on
C^{*}
.
We prefer to choose the sides so that left
C
-comodules become
left
C^{*}
-modules; then left
C
-contramodules also become left

C^{*}
-modules.

Given a graded left contramodule
P
over a graded coalgebra
C
,
a homogeneous element
p\in P
, and a homogeneous linear function

b\colon C\longrightarrow k
we let
b*p\in P
denote the result of the left
action of
b
on
p
.
We refer to
[
64
, Section 4.1]
for the sign rule.

Now we can present our definition.
Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
A
left CDG-contramodule

P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(P,d_{P})
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
a graded left
C
-contramodule endowed with

•

an odd contraderivation
d_{P}\colon P\longrightarrow P
of degree
1

compatible with the coderivation
d
on
C

such that

(vii)

the square of the differential
d_{P}
on
P
is described by
the formula
d_{P}^{2}(p)=h*p
for all
p\in P
.

Similarly to the theories of CDG-modules and CDG-comodules
(as in Sections
6.2
–
6.3
),
left CDG-contramodules over a CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
form
a DG-category
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.
Any morphism of CDG-coalgebras
(f,a)\colon(C,d_{C},h_{C})\longrightarrow(D,d_{D},h_{D})
induces a DG-functor
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow D^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

assigning to a CDG-contramodule
(P,d_{P})
the CDG-contramodule

(P,d^{\prime}_{P})
, with the graded
D
-contramodule structure on
P

obtained from the graded
C
-contramodule structure on
P
by
the contrarestriction of scalars (as mentioned in
Section
8.4
) and the twisted
differential
d^{\prime}_{P}
given by the rule
d^{\prime}_{P}(p)=d_{P}(p)+a*p
.
So an isomorphism of CDG-coalgebras induces an isomorphism of
the DG-categories of CDG-contramodules over them.

9.3.
Contraderived category of CDG-contramodules

This section is a contramodule version of
Sections
7.6
–
7.7
.
In the spirit of the discussion in the end of
Section
7.5
, we will see
(in Theorem
9.4
) that
the contraderived categories of contramodules are somewhat better
behaved than the contraderived categories of modules.
On the other hand, it makes
no
sense to consider
“coderived categories of contramodules”, as the functors of infinite
coproducts are usually
not
exact in contramodule categories
(contramodule categories of homological dimension
1
being
a remarkable exception; see
[
65
, Remark 1.2.1]
).

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Similarly to Sections
7.6
–
7.7
, one can speak of short exact
sequences
0\longrightarrow P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
of left
CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and their totalizations (total
CDG-contramodules)
\Tot(P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
,
which are again left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Let
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
denote the homotopy category of left
CDG-contramodules over a CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Similarly to Sections
7.6
–
7.7
, the DG-category
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

has shifts and cones, so its homotopy category
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})

is triangulated.

Definition 9.1

(
[
64
, Section 4.2]
)
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-coalgebra.
A left CDG-contramodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be

absolutely acyclic
if it belongs to the minimal thick subcategory
of
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
containing the totalizations of short exact
sequences of left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The full subcategory of absolutely acyclic CDG-contramodules is
denoted by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})

is called the
absolute derived category
of left
CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Definition 9.2

(
[
63
, Section 4.1]
,
[
64
, Section 4.2]
)
.

A left CDG-contramodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is said to be

contraacyclic
if it belongs to the minimal triangulated
subcategory of
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
containing the totalizations of
short exact sequences of left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

and closed under infinite products
.
The thick subcategory of contraacyclic CDG-contramodules is denoted
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
.
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})=\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})

is called the
contraderived category
of left CDG-contramodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

In the context of the next theorem, it is helpful to recall that
the homological dimensions of the three abelian categories of (ungraded
or graded) left
C
-comodules, right
C
-comodules, and left

C
-contramodules coincide for any (ungraded or graded) coalgebra
C

over a field
k
(see
[
64
, beginning of Section 4.5]
).
The common value of these three dimensions is called the
global
dimension
of the (ungraded or graded) coalgebra
C
.

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
whose
underlying graded coalgebra
C
has finite global dimension
(as a graded coalgebra; i. e., the abelian category of graded left

C
-contramodules has finite homological dimension).
Then the two classes of contraacyclic and absolutely acyclic
CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
coincide,

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}),

and accordingly, the two derived categories of the second kind coincide,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 4.5(b)]
.
For a generalization to exact DG-categories,
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.6(b) or Theorem 8.9(b)]
.
∎

Notice that the contramodule version of condition (
**
) from
Section
7.6
holds for

any
(graded) coalgebra
C
over a field
k
, because the class
of all projective
C
-contramodules is closed under infinite products.
Indeed, the projective
C
-contramodules are the direct summands of
the free ones (see Sections
8.1
–
8.2
), and free left
C
-contramodules have
the form
\Hom_{k}(C,V)
, where
V
ranges over (graded)
k
-vector
spaces; so free
C
-contramodules obviously form a class closed under
infinite products.

Similarly to Sections
7.6
–
7.7
, we denote by

\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\subset\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})

the full triangulated subcategory in the homotopy category formed by
all the CDG-contramodules whose underlying graded
C
-contramodules
are projective.
Such CDG-contramodules are called
graded-projective
.

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Then the composition
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
of the triangulated
inclusion functor
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})

and the Verdier quotient functor
\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})
is an equivalence of triangulated categories,

\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 4.4(d)]
.
For a generalization to exact DG-categories,
see
[
77
, Theorem 5.10(b)]
.
∎

9.4.
Derived co-contra correspondence

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C,d,h)
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Then there is a natural pair of adjoint DG-functors between
the DG-categories of left CDG-comodules and left CDG-contramodules
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The right adjoint DG-functor is

\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}},

while the left adjoint DG-functor is

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}\,\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}.

Let us spell out the constructions of these functors.
For any left CDG-comodule
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the underlying
graded left
C
-contramodule
\Hom_{C}(C,M)
of the CDG-contramodule

\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is simply the graded left
C
-contramodule of
homogeneous left
C
-comodule morphisms
C\longrightarrow M
.
This is the graded version of the construction of the functor

\Hom_{C}(C,{-})
from Section
8.7
.
So the degree
n
component
\Hom_{C}^{n}(C,M)
of the graded

C
-contramodule
\Hom_{C}(C,M)
is the vector space of all
homogeneous left
C
-comodule homomorphisms
C\longrightarrow M
of
degree
n
(for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
).
The differential
d
on the left CDG-contramodule

\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is given by the usual rule for the differential
on the Hom space of two precomplexes, as in
Sections
6.8

and
9.2
.

For any left CDG-contramodule
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the underlying
graded left
C
-comodule
C\odot_{C}P
of the CDG-comodule

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is simply the contratensor product of
the graded right
C
-comodule
C
and the graded left

C
-contramodule
P
.
Here one has to extend the construction of the contratensor product
functor from Section
8.6
to the realm
of graded coalgebras, graded comodules, and graded contramodules,
which is done in the most straightforward way
(see
[
64
, Section 2.2]
for the details).
The graded left
C
-comodule structure on the contratensor product

C\odot_{C}P
is induced by the graded left
C
-comodule structure
on
C
, as in Section
8.7
.
The differential
d
on the left CDG-comodule
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is given by the usual rule for the differential of the tensor product
of two precomplexes, as in Section
6.3
.

The standard notation for the comodule-contramodule correspondence
functors (as in
[
63
,
64
,
65
]
) is

\Psi_{C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}=\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
and
\Phi_{C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}=C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}
.

Theorem 9.5
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Then the adjoint DG-functors
\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
and

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}
, restricted to the full DG-subcategories of
graded-injective CDG-comodules and graded-projective
CDG-contramodules, induce a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}})\simeq\mathsf{Hot}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}).

Consequently, the right derived functor of
\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})

and the left derived functor of
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}
are mutually
inverse triangulated equivalences between the coderived and
the contraderived category,

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\,:\!C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}.

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 5.2]
.
To prove the first assertion, one observes that the adjunction
morphisms
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and

P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\>C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
are (closed)
isomorphisms of objects in the respective DG-categories for any
graded-injective left CDG-comodule
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and graded-projective
left CDG-contramodule
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
As the property of a closed morphism of CDG-comodules or
CDG-contramodules to be an isomorphism depends only on
the underlying morphism of graded co/contramodules, the claim
essentially follows from the graded version of the underived
co-contra correspondence theory of
Section
8.7
.
In other words, the DG-functors
\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
and

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}
are mutually inverse equivalences between
the DG-categories of graded-injective CDG-comodules and
graded-projective CDG-contramodules,
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
; hence they induce an equivalence of
the respective homotopy categories.

The second assertion follows from the first one in view of
Theorems
7.13

and
9.4
.
Essentially, the derived functor
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
is
constructed by applying the functor
\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
to
graded-injective CDG-comodules, while the derived functor

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
is constructed by applying the functor

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}{-}
to graded-projective CDG-contramodules.
∎

For references to further discussions of the philosophy and
generalizations of the co-contra correspondence, see the end of
Section
8.7
.

9.5.
Twisted differential on the graded Hom space

This section is the contramodule version of
Section
5.3
.
For the sake of simplicity of the exposition (to avoid
a detailed discussion of CDG-bimodules and CDG-bicomodules),
we restrict ourselves to the uncurved case.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-algebra and
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-coalgebra over
k
,
and let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a twisting cochain (as defined in
Section
5.1
).
Let
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(M,d_{M})
be a left DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and let

Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(Q,d_{Q})
be a left DG-contramodule over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Consider the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(M,Q)
of the underlying
graded vector spaces of
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and endow it with
the differential given by the formula

d(f)(x)=d_{Q}(f(x))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{M}(x))\pm d^{\tau}(f)

for all homogeneous
k
-linear maps
f\in\Hom_{k}^{|f|}(M,Q)
and
homogeneous elements
x\in M^{|x|}
.
Here
d^{\tau}\colon\Hom_{k}(M,Q)\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(M,Q)
is the homogeneous
map of degree
1
given by the rule

d^{\tau}(f)(x)=\pi(c\mapsto f(\tau(c)x)),

for all
f\in\Hom_{k}^{|f|}(M,Q)
and
x\in M^{|x|}
.
In this formula,
\pi\colon\Hom_{k}(C,Q)\longrightarrow Q
is the contraaction map,
while
c\longmapsto f(\tau(c)x)
is the homogeneous
k
-linear map

C\longrightarrow Q
assigning to an element
c\in C
the image of
the element
\tau(c)x\in M
under the map
f
.
The reader can consult with
[
64
, Section 6.2]
for
the sign rule.

Then one can check that
d^{2}(f)=0
.
So the graded vector space
\Hom_{k}(M,Q)
endowed with
the differential
d
is a complex.
We denote this complex by
\Hom^{\tau}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

Analogously, let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(N,d_{N})
be a left DG-comodule over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
and let
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(P,d_{P})
be a left DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Consider the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(N,P)
of the underlying
graded vector spaces of
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and endow it with
the differential given by the formula

d(f)(y)=d_{P}(f(y))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{N}(y))\pm d^{\tau}(f)

for all homogeneous
k
-linear maps
f\in\Hom_{k}^{|f|}(N,P)
and
homogeneous elements
y\in N^{|y|}
.
Here
d^{\tau}\colon\Hom_{k}(N,P)\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(N,P)
is the homogeneous
map of degree
1
given by the rule that, for any homogeneous
linear map
f\in\Hom_{k}^{|f|}(N,P)
, the map
d^{\tau}(f)\in\Hom_{k}^{|f|+1}(N,P)
is equal to the composition

N\overset{\nu}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}C\otimes_{k}N\overset{\tau\otimes f}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}A\otimes_{k}P\overset{n}{\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu}P

of the comultiplication map
\nu\colon N\longrightarrow C\otimes_{k}N
, the map

\tau\otimes f\colon C\otimes_{k}N\longrightarrow A\otimes_{k}P
, and the multiplication map

n\colon A\otimes_{k}P\longrightarrow P
.

Once again, choosing the sign properly
(cf.
[
64
, Section 6.2]
), one can check that
d^{2}(f)=0
.
So the graded vector space
\Hom_{k}(N,P)
endowed with
the differential
d
is a complex.
We denote this complex by
\Hom^{\tau}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

9.6.
Derived Koszul duality on the contramodule side

This section is the contramodule version of
Section
6.9
.
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-algebra and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-coalgebra over
k
,
and let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a twisting cochain
(as defined in Section
6.8
).

Given a left CDG-contramodule
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, we consider
the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(B,Q)
and endow it with
the differential
d
twisted with the twisting cochain
\tau
using
the same formulas as in
Section
9.5
.
Then
\Hom^{\tau}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=(\Hom_{k}(B,Q),\,d)
is a left
CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Here the left
B
-module structure on
B
has been eaten up in
the construction of the twisted differential on the Hom space,
but the right
B
-module structure on
B
remains and induces
the underlying graded left
B
-module structure of the CDG-module

\Hom^{\tau}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.

Similarly, given a left CDG-module
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, we
consider the graded Hom space
\Hom_{k}(C,P)
and endow it with
the differential
d
twisted with the twisting cochain
\tau
using
the same formulas as in
Section
9.5
.
Then
\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=(\Hom_{k}(C,P),\,d)
is a left
CDG-contramodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Here the left
C
-comodule structure on
C
has been consumed in
the construction of the twisted differential on the Hom space,
but the right
C
-comodule structure on
C
stays and induces
the underlying graded left
C
-contramodule structure of
the CDG-contramodule
\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.

Recall the notation
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
for the DG-category of left
CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
for the DG-category
of left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(see
Sections
6.2
and
9.2
).
Dually to Section
6.9
, one
observes that the DG-functor

\Hom^{\tau}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muB^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

is right adjoint to the DG-functor

\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a nonzero DG-algebra and
(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\gamma)
be
a conilpotent CDG-coalgebra over
k
(as defined in
Section
6.7
).
Let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be an acyclic twisting cochain
(as defined in Section
6.8
);
this includes the condition that
\tau\circ\gamma=0
.
Then the adjoint functors
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longmapsto\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

and
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longmapsto\Hom^{\tau}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
induce a triangulated
equivalence between the conventional
derived
category of
left DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and the
contraderived
category of
left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,

\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 6.5(b)]
.
The particular case corresponding to the twisting cochain from
Examples
6.8
can be
found in
[
64
, Theorem 6.3(b)]
, while
the case of the twisting cochain from
Example
6.9

(for a conilpotent CDG-coalgebra
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
)
is considered in
[
64
, Theorem 6.4(b)]
.
For the definition of the contraderived category,
see Section
9.3
above.
∎

The triangulated equivalence of
Theorem
9.6
takes
the left CDG-contramodule
k
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(with
the
C
-contramodule structure on
k
defined in terms of
the coaugmentation
\gamma
) to the cofree left DG-module

\Hom_{k}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},k)
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Notice that there is no natural structure of a DG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

on the one-dimensional vector space
k
, as the DG-algebra
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
not augmented.
This fact is not unrelated to the fact that there is no natural
structure of a left CDG-contramodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the free graded
left
C
-contramodule
\Hom_{k}(C,k)
(because of a mismatch of
the equations for the square of the differential involving
the curvature, similar to the one explained in the end of
Section
6.3
).

Theorem 9.7
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a nonzero CDG-coalgebra, and let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{w}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be the twisting cochain from
Example
6.10
.
Then the adjoint functors
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longmapsto\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})

and
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longmapsto\Hom^{\tau}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
induce a triangulated
equivalence between the
absolute derived
category of left
CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and the
contraderived
category of
left CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}).

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 6.7(b)]
.
The definitions of the contraderived and absolute derived categories
are explained in Sections
9.3

and
7.6
.
The absolute derived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
coincides with
the contraderived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})

by
[
64
, Theorem 3.6(a)]
; see
Theorem
7.8
(a) above.
∎

9.7.
Koszul triality

In this section, we summarize the results formulated above in this
survey in the form of commutative diagrams of Koszul triality.
There are two main triality theorems in
[
64
]
:
the conilpotent and the nonconilpotent one.

Theorem 9.8
.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a DG-algebra and
(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\gamma)
be
a conilpotent CDG-coalgebra over
k
.
Let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be an acyclic twisting cochain;
this includes the conditions that
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\neq 0
and
\tau\circ\gamma=0
.
Then there is a commutative diagram of triangulated category
equivalences

(7)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 27.26625pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise where the upper diagonal double line

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{D}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\,:\!A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}

;

9.6
; and
the vertical double line

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\,:\!C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}.

is the derived comodule-contramodule correspondence of
Theorem
9.5
.

Proof.

This is
[
64
, Theorem 6.5]
.
The particular case corresponding to the twisting cochain from
Examples
6.8
can be
found in
[

, Theorem 6.3]
, while
the case of the twisting cochain from
Example

(for a conilpotent CDG-coalgebra

)
is considered in
[

, Theorem 6.4]
.
For the definitions of the coderived and the contraderived category,

and
9.3
.
For discussions of some elements of the proof of the theorem,
see Remarks

and

below.
∎

Here the comodule side of the Koszul duality takes
the left CDG-comodule
k
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(with the
C
-comodule
structure on
k
defined in terms of the coaugmentation
\gamma
)
to the free left DG-module
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The contramodule side of the Koszul duality takes
the left CDG-contramodule
k
over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(with
the
C
-contramodule structure on
k
similarly defined in terms of
the coaugmentation
\gamma
) to the cofree left DG-module

\Hom_{k}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},k)
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Now let us assume that
(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\alpha)
is an augmented
DG-algebra and
(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\gamma)
is a conilpotent DG-coalgebra.
Let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be an acyclic twisting cochain
satisfying the equation
\alpha\circ\tau=0
(in addition to
the previously assumed
\tau\circ\gamma=0
).
For example, the twisting cochains from
Examples
5.1
,

5.2
,
and
5.3
, as well as
generally “acyclic twisting cochains” in the sense of
Section
5.2
,
satisfy these additional conditions.

Then the left DG-module
k
over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
(with the
A
-module
structure on
k
defined in terms of the augmentation
\alpha
),
the cofree left DG-comodule
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and the free
left DG-contramodule
\Hom_{k}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},k)
over
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
correspond
to each other under the triality (
7
) of
Theorem
9.8
.

Theorem 9.9
.

Let
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\neq 0
be a CDG-coalgebra, and let
\tau\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\Cb^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{w}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})=B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be the twisting cochain from
Example
6.10
.
Then there is a commutative diagram of triangulated category
equivalences

(8)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 46.08168pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{{\mathsf{co}}={\mathsf{abs}}={\mathsf{ctr}}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.1917pt\raise 0.98146pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.1917pt\raise-0.98146pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.1917pt\raise 0.98146pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.1917pt\raise-0.98146pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.1917pt\raise 0.98146pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern where the upper diagonal double line

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{D}^{{\mathsf{abs}}={\mathsf{co}}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\,:\!B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}

;

\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon\mathsf{D}^{{\mathsf{abs}}={\mathsf{ctr}}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\,:\!\Hom^{\tau}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})

is the contramodule side nonconilpotent Koszul duality of
Theorem
9.7
; and
the vertical double line

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{C}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})\colon\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}})\,:\!C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\odot_{C}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}.

is the derived comodule-contramodule correspondence of

9.5
.

, Theorem 6.7]
.

,

,
and
9.3
.
The notation
\mathsf{D}^{{\mathsf{co}}={\mathsf{abs}}={\mathsf{ctr}}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
is intended to
emphasize that the coderived, contraderived, and absolute derived

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
coincide by
Theorem
7.8
(a) (since the free
graded algebra
B
has finite global dimension
1
).
For a discussion of some elements of the proof of the theorem,
see Remark
7.15

and Section
9.8
.
∎

9.8.
Some comments on the proof

The aim of this section is to complement the proofs of the Koszul
duality theorems
[
64
, Theorems 6.3, 6.4, 6.5, and 6.7]

(see Theorems
9.8

and
9.9
above)
with some additional details.

In the context of both the diagrams (
7
)
and (
8
), there are three points to be
explained: that the diagonal (Koszul duality) functors are well-defined,
that the diagonal functors acting in the opposite directions are
mutually inverse, and that the triangular diagram is commutative.
We have already commented upon the vertical (co-contra) equivalence in
the proof of Theorem
9.5
.

The main thing to observe is that
the Koszul duality functors are
exact and need not be derived
.
There is
no
need to replace a (C)DG-module, CDG-comodule, or
CDG-contramodule with any resolution (adjusted version) of it before
applying any one of the functors
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
,
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
, etc. (defined in
Sections
6.9

and
9.6
).

In fact, the Koszul duality functors have even better properties:
they
take weakly trivial objects to contractible ones
.
For example, in the context of the conilpotent duality of
Theorem
9.8
, the functor

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
takes coacyclic CDG-comodules to contractible
DG-modules, while the functor
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
takes acyclic
DG-modules to contractible CDG-comodules.
Similarly, in the same theorem the functor
\Hom^{\tau}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})

takes contraacyclic CDG-contramodules to contractible DG-modules,
while the functor
\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
takes acyclic DG-modules
to contractible CDG-contramodules.

By adjunction, the same property of the Koszul duality functors can be
expressed by saying that they
take arbitrary objects to adjusted
ones
(meaning “adjusted” on the suitable side and for the respective
exotic derived category).
For example, in the context of
Theorem
9.8
, the functor

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
takes arbitrary CDG-comodules to homotopy projective
DG-modules, while the functor
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
takes arbitrary
DG-modules to graded-injective CDG-comodules.
Similarly, in the same theorem the functor
\Hom^{\tau}(A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})

takes arbitrary CDG-contramodules to homotopy injective DG-modules,
while the functor
\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
takes arbitrary DG-modules
to graded-projective CDG-contramodules.

The fact that the Koszul duality functors take co/contraacyclic
objects to contractible ones is explained in
Remark
7.15
.
Let us make a comment on the proofs of the claims that the functor

C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes^{\tau}{-}
takes acyclic DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
to
contractible CDG-comodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, while the functor

\Hom^{\tau}(C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},{-})
takes acyclic DG-modules over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

to contractible CDG-contramodules over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(in the context of
the conilpotent Koszul duality of
Theorem
9.8
).
To complement the arguments in
[
64
, Sections 6.3 and 6.4]

and Remark
7.1
, let us spell out
one basic observation.

For the sake of accessibility of the exposition, we formulate
the following lemma for CDG-modules, while in fact parts (a), (b)
are equally applicable to CDG-comodules and parts (a), (c) to
CDG-contramodules.

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring.

(a)
Let
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
endowed with
a finite filtration
0=F_{0}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset F_{1}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset\dotsb\subset F_{n}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
by CDG-submodules
F_{i}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Assume that the CDG-module
F_{i}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F_{i-1}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
absolutely acyclic for every
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then the CDG-module
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is absolutely acyclic.

(b)
Let
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
endowed with
an increasing filtration
0=F_{0}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset F_{1}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset\dotsb\subset F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset F_{i+1}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset\dotsb
by CDG-submodules

F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Assume that the filtration
F
is exhaustive, that is

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and the CDG-module

F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F_{i-1}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic for every
i\geq 1
.
Then the CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic.

(c)
Let
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
endowed with
a decreasing filtration
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=F^{0}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\supset F^{1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\supset\dotsb\supset F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\supset F^{i+1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\supset\dotsb
by CDG-submodules

F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Assume that the filtration
F
is (separated and) complete, that is

Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\varprojlim_{i\geq 1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and the CDG-module

F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i+1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contraacyclic for every
i\geq 0
.
Then the CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contraacyclic.

Proof.

This lemma is implicit in
[
63
,
64
]
; cf.
[
63
, proof of
Lemma 2.1]
or
[
64
, proof of Theorem 3.7]
.
Part (a): proceeding by induction on
n
, it suffices to prove that,
for any short exact sequence of CDG-modules
0\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the CDG-module
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
absolutely acyclic whenever both the CDG-modules
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

are.
Indeed, the CDG-module
T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\Tot(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\to N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
is
absolutely acyclic by the definition, and the CDG-module
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
homotopy equivalent to a CDG-module which can be obtained from
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,

T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
using (a small finite number of) passages to
shifts and cones.

Part (b): similarly to part (a), one proves by induction on
i
that
the CDG-module
F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic for every
i\geq 1
.
Having that established, consider the telescope short exact sequence

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muN^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

of CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and denote by
T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
its total
CDG-module.
Then the CDG-module
T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is absolutely acyclic by the definition,
while the CDG-module
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\infty}F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic
since the CDG-modules
F_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are.
It follows that the CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic.

Part (c): similarly to part (a), one proves by induction on
i
that
the CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contraacyclic for every
i\geq 1
.
Now one can consider the telescope short exact sequence

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muQ^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

of CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
This sequence is exact, because the transition maps in the projective
system
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i+1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are surjective and

Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\varprojlim_{i\geq 1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Denote by
T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
its total CDG-module.
Then the CDG-module
T^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is absolutely acyclic by the definition,
while the CDG-module
\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
contraacyclic since the CDG-modules
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/F^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are.
It follows that the CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contraacyclic.
∎

Our next comment is on the proofs of the claims that the diagonal
functors in the opposite directions are mutually inverse in
Theorems
9.8

and
9.9
.
The following observations are intended to complement the arguments
in the proofs of
[
64
, Theorems 4.4, 6.3, and 6.4]
.
As above, we formulate the assertions for CDG-modules, though those
of them not involving contraacyclicity are also applicable to
CDG-comodules, while those not involving coacyclicity are applicable
to CDG-contramodules.

Lemma 9.11
.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B,d,h)
be a CDG-ring and
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M,d_{M})
be a left
CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Let
L\subset M
be a graded
B
-submodule.
Assume that the composition
L\rightarrowtail M\overset{d_{M}}{\longrightarrow}M\twoheadrightarrow M/L
is an isomorphism of graded abelian groups
(or of graded left
B
-modules)
L\longrightarrow(M/L)[1]
.
Then the CDG-module
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contractible.

Proof.

Here the notation
L\longrightarrow(M/L)[1]
means that the composition of
maps in question is a homogeneous map
L\longrightarrow M/L
of degree
1
.
In fact, it is always a morphism of graded left
B
-modules (with
the suitable sign rule).
The map
L\longrightarrow(M/L)[1]
being bijective means that the CDG-module

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
freely generated
by the graded left

B
-module
L
.
In the notation of
[
64
, proof of Theorem 3.6]
, this is
expressed by the formula
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=G^{+}(L)
.
All CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
freely generated by graded
B
-modules
are easily seen to be contractible.
∎

Now let us consider a CDG-module
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
endowed with
a filtration by graded
B
-submodules

\dotsb\subset F_{-i}M\subset F_{-i+1}M\subset\dotsb\subset F_{0}M\subset F_{1}M\subset\dotsb\subset F_{i}M\subset F_{i+1}M\subset\dotsb

The differential
d_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow M
is
not
supposed to preserve
this filtration; however, we assume that
d_{M}(F_{i}M)\subset F_{i+1}M
.
Then for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have a morphism of graded left

B
-modules
F_{i}M/F_{i-1}M\longrightarrow(F_{i+1}M/F_{i}M)[1]
induced by
d_{M}
.
The collection of all such maps is a
complex of graded left

B
-modules

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F_{i-1}M/F_{i-2}M)[i-1]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F_{i}M/F_{i-1}M)[i]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F_{i+1}M/F_{i}M)[i+1]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb

Lemma 9.12
.

(a)
Let
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N,d_{N})
be a left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
and let
0=F_{0}N\subset F_{1}N\subset F_{2}N\subset\dotsb
be an exhaustive
increasing filtration of
N
by graded
B
-submodules
F_{i}N\subset N
.
Assume that
d_{N}(F_{i}N)\subset F_{i+1}N
for all
i\geq 1
and
the complex of graded left
B
-modules

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muF_{1}N[1]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F_{2}N/F_{1}N)[2]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F_{3}N/F_{2}N)[3]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb

with the differential induced by
d_{N}
is acyclic.
Then the CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic.

(b)
Let
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(Q,d_{Q})
be a left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
and let
Q=F^{0}Q\supset F^{1}Q\supset F^{2}Q\supset\dotsb
be a complete
decreasing filtration of
Q
by graded
B
-submodules
F^{i}Q\subset Q
.
Assume that
d_{Q}(F^{i}Q)\subset F^{i-1}Q
for all
i\geq 2
and
the complex of graded left
B
-modules

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F^{2}Q/F^{3}Q)[-2]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(F^{1}Q/F^{2}Q)[-1]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muQ/F^{1}Q\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

with the differential induced by
d_{Q}
is acyclic.
Then the CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is contraacyclic.

Proof.

Part (a): define a new increasing filtration
G
on
N
by the rule

G_{i}N=F_{i}N+d_{N}(F_{i}N)
.
Then
0=G_{0}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset G_{1}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset G_{2}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset\dotsb
is
an increasing filtration of
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
by CDG-submodules
G_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\subset N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The filtration
G
is exhaustive if and only if the filtration
F
is.
In view of Lemma
9.11
, the condition
of exactness of the complex of graded left
B
-modules in part (a)
implies that the CDG-modules
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{i}=G_{i}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/G_{i-1}N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are
contractible for all
i\geq 1
.
According to Lemma
9.10
(b),
it follows that the CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is coacyclic.
The proof of part (b) is dual and based on
Lemmas
9.11

and
9.10
(c): the new filtration

G^{i}Q=F^{i+1}Q+d_{Q}(F^{i+1}Q)
is a decreasing filtration by
CDG-submodules of
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, it is complete if and only if the filtration

F
is, the condition of exactness of the complex implies that
the CDG-modules
G^{i}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}/G^{i+1}Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are contractible, etc.
∎
